Finally we emphasize that in general fermionic and parafermionic theories, the chiral parity symmetries discussed above are often absent (e.g. broken by interactions). In these cases, the general relations between bosonic and (para)fermionic theories in (
2.28
) and (
2.36
) still apply, but the bosonic theory
\mathcal{T}_{B}
and its orbifold
\mathcal{T}_{B^{\prime}}
are now distinct. On the
c=1
moduli space, this happens for example on the circle branch at
R=\sqrt{3}\,,2\sqrt{3}
where the theories are denoted by
\mathcal{N}=2
super-Ising
±
in Figure
2
, and also on the orbifold branch at the same radii described by
\mathcal{N}=1
super-Ising
±
.
9
9

9

Here the orbifold by
{\mathbb{Z}}_{2}^{C}
of the
\mathcal{N}=2
super-Ising
±
CFT projects out half of the supercurrents, leaving behind an
\mathcal{N}=1
super-Virasoro symmetry.

The underlying fermionic theories correspond to
\mathcal{N}=2
and
\mathcal{N}=1
super-Virasoro minimal models at
c=1
respectively. The
\pm
subscripts are interchanged by stacking with the
(-1)^{\rm Arf(\rho)}
SPT which leads to different fermionic and bosonic theories in these cases.

3
Fusion Categories of the Compact Free Boson

In this section we explore the fusion category symmetries of the
c=1
compact boson along the circle branch. At radius
R=\sqrt{2k}
, these theories are rational with an enhanced chiral algebra
U(1)_{2k}
. We discuss the associated Verlinde lines in Section
3.2
, which for this chiral algebra are all invertible. Also at this radius, the theory is self-dual under gauging a
\mathbb{Z}_{k}
symmetry, and there is an associated Tambara-Yamagami symmetry (which is beyond the Verlinde lines), which we discuss in Section
3.3
. Finally, at the radii
k\sqrt{2}
, which are obtained by gauging a
\mathbb{Z}_{k}
symmetry of the
SU(2)_{1}
theory, the enhanced continuous symmetry of
SU(2)_{1}
gives rise to a continuum of TDLs we discuss in Section
3.5
.

3.1
Operator Content of the Compact Boson

There are several ways of describing the compact free boson in 1+1D, which we call the compact boson for short. The most direct description is in terms of a
2\pi R
-periodic field
X
, where
R
is the radius of the target space circle. We define the
2\pi
-periodic field
\theta=X/R
and its
2\pi
-periodic conjugate momentum
\phi
. They may be expressed in terms of the left and right moving fields
X_{L,R}
as
10
10

10

We note there is an important subtlety which is that while it is possible to define
\theta
and
\phi
as
2\pi
-periodic, there is no way to consistently assign periodicities to
X_{L}
and
X_{R}
, because the above change of variables is not invertible over
\mathbb{Z}
for any
R
. This may be accounted for by introducing certain cocycles into the definition of the vertex operators (see Section 5 of
[
57
]
). One can avoid this subtlety by working with
\theta
and
\phi
but to make contact with the CFT literature we include transformation rules for
X_{L}
and
X_{R}
as well, with the caveat that these rules only unambiguously define the symmetry actions on the oscillator part of the spectrum.

\displaystyle\theta=R^{-1}(X_{L}+X_{R})\,,\quad\phi=R(X_{L}-X_{R})/2\,.

(3.1)

The compact boson at a generic radius
R
has symmetry

\displaystyle G_{\rm bos}=(U(1)^{\theta}\times U(1)^{\phi})\rtimes\mathbb{Z}_{2}^{C}\,,

(3.2)

where
U(1)^{\theta}
and
U(1)^{\phi}
act as shift symmetries of
\theta
and
\phi
, respectively, and “charge conjugation”
C
acts as

\displaystyle C:\begin{cases}\theta\mapsto-\theta\,,\\
\phi\mapsto-\phi\,.\end{cases}

(3.3)

The primary local operators in this theory consist of vertex operators

\displaystyle\begin{gathered}V_{n,w}=:e^{i\left({n\over R}+{wR\over 2}\right)X_{L}}e^{i\left({n\over R}-{wR\over 2}\right)X_{R}}:=:e^{in\theta}e^{iw\phi}:\qquad n,w\in\mathbb{Z}\,,\\
(h,\bar{h})=\left(\frac{1}{2}\left(\frac{n}{R}+\frac{wR}{2}\right)^{2},\frac{1}{2}\left(\frac{n}{R}-\frac{wR}{2}\right)^{2}\right),\end{gathered}

(3.4)

where
(h,\bar{h})
label the conformal weights,
as well as normal ordered Schur (symmetric) polynomials in the
U(1)
currents
j_{1}=\partial X_{L}
and
\bar{j}_{1}=\bar{\partial}X_{R}
and their derivatives, which we denote by

\displaystyle j_{n^{2}}\bar{j}_{m^{2}}\qquad(h,\bar{h})=(n^{2},m^{2})\,.

(3.5)

There is a duality known as T-duality which takes

\displaystyle\begin{gathered}R\mapsto 2/R\,,\\
X_{R}\mapsto-X_{R}\,,\\
\theta\leftrightarrow\phi\,,\end{gathered}

(3.6)

and will play a crucial role in our story. As written, this duality commutes with charge conjugation
C
and exchanges
U(1)^{\theta}
and
U(1)^{\phi}
.

There are several important subtleties of this duality, emphasized in
[
57
]
, which can be seen at the self-dual radius
R=\sqrt{2}
. At this radius, there is an enhanced
SO(4)=(SU(2)_{L}\times SU(2)_{R})/\mathbb{Z}_{2}
symmetry in which we expect to find T-duality. To define it unambiguously we need to specify the
SO(4)
matrix by which it acts. The following specifies the vector representation of the
SO(4)
,

(\cos\theta,\sin\theta,\cos\phi,\sin\phi)\,,

(3.7)

and in this basis we choose the swapping matrix

T=\begin{bmatrix}0&&I_{2\times 2}\\
I_{2\times 2}&&0\end{bmatrix}\,,

(3.8)

which agrees with the usual prescription (
3.6
). This differs from the choice in
[
57
]
, which is order 4 and anomalous. The above is anomaly-free, and generalize to self-dualities at the other points on the circle branch, as we will see.

3.2
RCFTs on the circle branch

Special points on the
c=1
moduli space (see Figure
2
) are described by rational CFTs, which have a finite number of conformal blocks with respect to an enhanced chiral algebra. These arise when the radius satisfies
R^{2}\in\mathbb{Q}
. For these theories we can study the Verlinde lines, which turn out to be invertible, and generate a finite subgroup of the ordinary global symmetry.

We start with the case
R=\sqrt{2k}
for
k\in{\mathbb{Z}}
. The enhanced chiral algebra is generated by

\displaystyle T\,,~j_{L}\,,~e^{\pm i\sqrt{2k}X_{L}}

(3.9)

of spin
2,1,k
respectively, and likewise for the right-movers. We will refer to this as the
U(1)_{2k}
chiral algebra (the associated 3d Chern-Simons theory is
U(1)_{2k}
). There are
2k
chiral primaries as listed in Table
5
.

Primary

e^{in\theta}

(n=-k+1,-k+2,\dots,k)

h

{n^{2}\over 4k}

Characters

K^{k}_{n}(\tau)\equiv{1\over\eta(\tau)}\sum_{r\in{\mathbb{Z}}}q^{k\left(r+{n\over 2k}\right)^{2}}

Table 5:
The
2k
chiral primaries in
U(1)_{2k}
.

The
U(1)_{2k}
characters satisfy the following identities

\displaystyle K_{m}^{k}=K_{-m}^{k}=K^{k}_{2k+m}\,.

(3.10)

Under the modular
S
transformation, we have

\displaystyle K_{n}^{k}(-1/\tau)={1\over\sqrt{2k}}\sum_{m\in{\mathbb{Z}}_{2k}}e^{-{\pi imn\over k}}K_{m}^{k}(\tau)\,,

(3.11)

while under the
T
transformation, they become

\displaystyle K_{n}^{k}(\tau+1)=e^{\pi in^{2}\over 2k}e^{-{\pi i\over 12}}K_{n}^{k}(\tau)\,.

(3.12)

The partition function of the theory is given by the diagonal modular invariant

\displaystyle Z_{\rm bos}(R=\sqrt{2k})=\sum_{m=-k+1}^{k}|K^{k}_{m}|^{2}

(3.13)

and we will refer to this theory as the
U(1)_{2k}
CFT.

The Verlinde lines in this theory are labelled by the primaries
e^{in\theta}
,
n\in\mathbb{Z}_{2k}
. Let us denote the corresponding line by
\mathcal{L}_{n}
. As for any Verlinde line, the action of
\mathcal{L}_{n}
on local operators is determined by the modular
S
matrix, such that an operator in the
|K_{m}^{k}|^{2}
block gets a phase
e^{-i\pi mn/k}
. We recognize this action as the
\mathbb{Z}_{2k}
shift symmetry
\theta\mapsto\theta-\pi m/k
.

These lines are all invertible. Indeed, the Chern-Simons theory
U(1)_{2k}
which governs the properties of these lines is abelian. In the following, we will find there are also non-invertible lines at these and other special radii.

The more general RCFTs on the circle branch are associated to non-diagonal modular invariants of the chiral algebra for
U(1)_{2k}
. The RCFT at
R=\sqrt{2p\over q}
with positive coprime integers
p,q
has torus partition function

\displaystyle Z_{\rm bos}\left(R=\sqrt{2p\over q}\right)=\sum_{r=0}^{2q-1}\sum_{s=0}^{2p-1}K^{pq}_{pr+qs}(\tau)K^{pq}_{pr-qs}(\bar{\tau})\,,

(3.14)

which can be easily derived using the fact that this CFT is the
{\mathbb{Z}}_{q}
(shift symmetry) orbifold of the
U(1)_{2pq}
CFT. A
{\mathbb{Z}}_{2p}
subset of the
{\mathbb{Z}}_{2pq}
Verlinde lines in the
U(1)_{2pq}
CFT survives the orbifold and becomes a part of the global symmetry in the non-diagonal RCFT at
R=\sqrt{2p\over q}
.
11
11

11

General TDLs that are transparent to the chiral algebra in a non-diagonal RCFT can be identified using the corresponding 3d TQFT on a slab with a surface operator parallel to the boundaries that defines the modular invariant
[
7
,
8
,
58
]
. Here the TDLs come from bulk anyons and their fusion products in the presence of the surface operator
[
17
]
. The corresponding map between the fusion category symmetries underlying the diagonal and non-diagonal RCFT is known as
the
{\alpha}
-induction
[
59
,
60
,
61
,
62
,
63
,
64
]
.

3.3
\mathbb{Z}_{k}
Tambara-Yamagami symmetry

The Verlinde lines discussed above on the circle branch are all invertible. There are also non-invertible TDLs at these radii, indicated by the self-duality under
\mathbb{Z}_{k}
gauging, which as we have discussed in Section
2.4
, gives rise to a
\mathbb{Z}_{k}
Tambara-Yamagami (TY) symmetry. Recall the
\mathbb{Z}_{k}
TY category is characterized by a nondegenerate bicharacter
\mathbb{Z}_{k}\times\mathbb{Z}_{k}\to U(1)
, which may be written as

\displaystyle e^{i\chi(a,b)}=\omega^{ab}\,,

(3.15)

with
\omega
a primitive
k
-th root of unity, as well as the Frobenius-Schur indicator
\epsilon=\pm 1
of the duality defect
\mathcal{N}

[
36
]
. In this subsection, we will constrain this data and identify the TY symmetry action from knowledge of the operator spectrum on the
c=1
circle branch. We find an continuum of such symmetry actions parametrized by a sign
\pm
and a number
\alpha\in[0,1)
,

\displaystyle\widehat{\mathcal{N}}_{{\alpha},\pm}:\begin{cases}V_{n,w}\to\sqrt{k}e^{\pi inw+2\pi i\alpha(n\mp kw)}V_{\pm wk,\pm{n\over k}}\quad{~\rm for~}n\in k{\mathbb{Z}}\,,\\
(\partial X_{L},\bar{\partial}X_{R})\to(\pm\sqrt{k}\partial X_{L},\mp\sqrt{k}\bar{\partial}X_{R})\,,\\
{\rm other\ primaries}\to{\rm non-local\ operators}\,,\end{cases}

(3.16)

where
V_{n,w}
are the vertex operators defined in (
3.4
). The corresponding fusion category is

\displaystyle\mathcal{N}_{{\alpha},\pm}\to{\rm TY}({\mathbb{Z}}_{k},\chi_{\pm},1)\,,

(3.17)

where
\chi_{\pm}(a,b)\equiv\pm{2\pi ab\over k}
, as we will derive in the following sections.

3.3.1
Self-duality under gauging

Let us consider the (anomaly-free) cyclic
\mathbb{Z}_{k}^{\theta}
subgroup of
U(1)^{\theta}
, which acts as

\mathbb{Z}_{k}^{\theta}:\theta\mapsto\theta+\frac{2\pi}{k}\,.

(3.18)

We can think of this as a
k
-fold rotation symmetry of the circle target space. It is known that when one gauges a discrete geometric symmetry of a sigma model, if that symmetry acts freely on the target space, the result is again a sigma model but now the target space is replaced by its quotient
[
27
]
, in this case a circle of radius
R\over k
. Indeed, when we gauge this subgroup, we restrict the
\theta
vertex operators to have momentum numbers
n=k\tilde{n}
, while the twisted sectors contribute
\phi
vertex operators of fractional winding
w=\tilde{w}/k
. Comparing with (
3.4
), we see the spectrum of the
V_{k\tilde{n},\tilde{w}/k}
at radius
R
matches that of the
V_{\tilde{n},\tilde{w}}
at radius
R\over k
.

At the special radius
R=\sqrt{2k}
,
{R\over k}=\sqrt{2\over k}
is the T-dual radius, so this theory is self-dual under gauging
\mathbb{Z}_{k}^{\theta}
. As in (
2.15
), one consequence of this self-duality is an identity of twisted partition functions

\displaystyle Z_{\rm bos}(R=\sqrt{2k},\Sigma,B)={1\over\sqrt{|H^{1}(\Sigma,\mathbb{Z}_{k})|}}\sum_{A\in H^{1}(\Sigma,\mathbb{Z}_{k})}Z_{\rm bos}(R=\sqrt{2k},\Sigma,A)\omega^{\int_{\Sigma}A\cup B}\,,

(3.19)

where
A,B\in H^{1}(\Sigma,\mathbb{Z}_{k})
are background
\mathbb{Z}_{k}
gauge fields on the spacetime manifold
\Sigma
,
\cup
denotes the cup product of gauge fields, and
\omega
is a primitive
k
-th root of unity which defines the bicharacter for the TY category.

Let us now determine the possible
\omega
which can occur in this formula for the theory at hand. Indeed an immediate consequence of the self-duality condition (
3.19
) applied to the
U(1)_{2k}
CFT on
T^{2}
is that
\omega=e^{\pm{2\pi i\over k}}
. This follows by studying the coefficients of the term
q^{{1\over 4k}-{1\over 24}}\bar{q}^{{1\over 4k}-{1\over 24}}
on the two sides of the equality, with
B
chosen to have holonomy equal to the
\xi:\theta\to\theta+{2\pi\over k}
generator of
{\mathbb{Z}}_{k}^{\theta}
around the temporal cycle, and trivial holonomy around the spatial cycle. On the LHS, the two momentum operators
V_{\pm 1,0}
contribute
e^{\pm{2\pi i\over k}}
for a total of
2\cos{2\pi\over k}
. On the RHS, this term comes from the
{\mathbb{Z}}_{k}^{\theta}
symmetry generator
\xi
acting on twisted sector operators. Now the
\xi^{n}
-twisted sector ground states for
0\leq n\leq k-1
correspond to operators with fractional winding
e^{{in\over k}\phi}
or
e^{{i(n-k)\over k}\phi}
depending on which one has the lower scaling dimension. Clearly
h=\bar{h}={1\over 4k}
is only attainable for
n=1,k-1
. Furthermore since these operators are scalars, whereby the
T
-transformation acts trivially, they carry vanishing
{\mathbb{Z}}_{k}^{\theta}
charge. Meanwhile the bicharacter contributes
\omega
and
\omega^{k-1}=\bar{\omega}
, respectively. Consequently, we arrive at the promised equality

\displaystyle 2\cos{2\pi i\over k}=\omega+\bar{\omega}\,,

(3.20)

which determines
\omega
up to a conjugation ambiguity.

Generally speaking, there is a certain ambiguity in the expression (
3.19
), whereby the Galois action on
\omega
can be equated with the action of
{\rm Aut}(\mathbb{Z}_{k}^{\theta})
on the background gauge field
B
. The partition function of the compact boson is invariant under
B\mapsto-B
because of the charge conjugation symmetry
C
, which acts to negate the generator of
\mathbb{Z}_{k}^{\theta}
. One can check that it has no further
{\rm Aut}(\mathbb{Z}_{k}^{\theta})
ambiguities by studying the operators
V_{\pm 1,0}
with momentum charge
\pm 1
and zero winding charge. Thus, the identity above cannot distinguish between
\omega
and
\bar{\omega}
. In fact, by fusing a
\mathbb{Z}_{k}
TY TDL with a certain bicharacter
e^{i\chi}
by the
C
TDL we obtain a
\mathbb{Z}_{k}
TY TDL with the conjugate bicharacter
e^{-i\chi}
, so they always come in pairs (see Figure
13
).

When we study the TDL action on local operators in the CFT, there are new constraints on
\omega
. We will analyze these constraints using the duality twisted partition functions.

3.3.2
Duality acting on local operators

The duality is obtained by gauging and then applying T-duality. Using the definition of T-duality in (
3.6
), we obtain the following action of the TDL
\mathcal{N}
on the primaries

\displaystyle\widehat{\mathcal{N}}:\begin{cases}V_{n,w}\to\sqrt{k}e^{\pi inw}V_{wk,{n\over k}}\quad{~\rm for~}n\in k{\mathbb{Z}}\,,\\
(\partial X_{L},\bar{\partial}X_{R})\to(\sqrt{k}\partial X_{L},-\sqrt{k}\bar{\partial}X_{R})\,,\\
{\rm other\ primaries}\to\text{non-local primaries}\,,\end{cases}

(3.21)

corresponding to the special case
\widehat{\mathcal{N}}_{{\alpha}=0,+}
in (
3.16
).
The extra phase
e^{\pi inw}
comes from the mutual non-locality of
\theta
and
\phi
variables. It is easy to check that
\widehat{\mathcal{N}}
satisfies the
\mathbb{Z}_{k}
TY fusion rule

\widehat{\mathcal{N}}^{2}=\sum_{m\in\mathbb{Z}_{k}}U^{\theta}(2\pi m/k)\,,

(3.22)

where
U^{\theta}(2\pi m/k)
generates a
2\pi m/k
shift of
\theta
.

This solution is not unique—there are actually many
\mathbb{Z}_{k}
duality defects in the
U(1)_{2k}
CFT. For instance we can compose
\mathcal{N}
with the charge conjugation symmetry
C
(which as we mentioned above yields a
\mathbb{Z}_{k}
duality TDL
C\mathcal{N}
with the conjugate bicharacter). Generally besides this
C
ambiguity we can also modify the action of
\mathcal{N}
on the operators
V_{n,w}
(
3.21
) by a phase
\mu(n,w)
. Consistency with the OPE and the TY fusion rule requires that
\mu(n,w)=e^{2\pi i{\alpha}(n-kw)}
for
{\alpha}\in[0,1)
. This phase corresponds to an invertible defect

\displaystyle\mathcal{L}^{U(1)_{R}}_{{\alpha}}\equiv e^{i{\alpha}\sqrt{2k}\oint d\bar{z}\bar{\partial}X_{R}}\,,

(3.23)

associated to the right-moving
U(1)
symmetry, and we can write the modified duality defect as
\mathcal{N}_{{\alpha},+}\equiv\mathcal{N}\mathcal{L}^{U(1)_{R}}_{{\alpha}}
. It is easy to check that it satisfies the
{\mathbb{Z}}_{k}
TY fusion rule as a consequence of (
3.21
). Similarly, we have the modified TY duality defect
\mathcal{N}_{{\alpha},-}\equiv C\mathcal{N}\mathcal{L}^{U(1)_{L}}_{{\alpha}}

using the left-moving
U(1)
symmetry.
Therefore the
U(1)_{2k}
RCFT contains a family of
{\mathbb{Z}}_{k}
TY categories indexed by
{\alpha}
(and a complementary family related by
C
).
12
12

12

\bar{\mathcal{N}}_{\alpha,\pm}\mathcal{N}_{\alpha,\pm}=\mathcal{N}_{\alpha,\pm}\mathcal{N}_{\alpha,\pm}
contains the trivial line, and the Noether current obtained from this family by the method of Section
2.5
is just the local
U(1)_{R}
current
\bar{\partial}X_{R}
.
Since they are related by invertible defects which are already known, it is enough to show that just one of these symmetry actions in (
3.16
) is associated with a TDL, so we will focus on the case
\mathcal{N}=\mathcal{N}_{{\alpha}=0,+}
below.
13
13

13

As we will see, the compositions with the chiral symmetries
\mathcal{L}^{U(1)_{R}}_{{\alpha}}
and
\mathcal{L}^{U(1)_{L}}_{{\alpha}}
do not lead to further changes to the TY category.

Let us study the torus partition function of the
U(1)_{2k}
CFT with the
\mathcal{N}
TDL inserted along a spatial cycle. For even
k
,
\mathcal{N}
commutes with the left-moving chiral algebra, and its action on the right-moving side projects to the sector with
n=wk
, namely the vacuum block with respect to the chiral algebra.
14
14

14

As a consequence, the duality defect
\mathcal{N}\mathcal{L}_{\alpha}^{U(1)_{R}}
has the identical twisted partition function as for
\mathcal{N}
.
Therefore we have

\displaystyle Z_{1\mathcal{N}}(\tau,\bar{\tau})=

\displaystyle\sqrt{k}K_{0}^{k}(\tau)\left(\sum_{m\in{\mathbb{Z}}_{\geq 0}}(-1)^{m}\chi_{m^{2}}(\bar{\tau})\right)={\sqrt{k}\over|\eta(\tau)|^{2}}\sum_{n,m\in{\mathbb{Z}}}(-1)^{m}q^{kn^{2}}\bar{q}^{m^{2}}={\sqrt{k}\theta_{3}(2k\tau)\theta_{4}(2\bar{\tau})\over|\eta|^{2}}\,,

(3.24)

where
K_{0}^{k}
is the identity character for the
U(1)_{2k}
chiral algebra (see Table
5
).
15
15

15

In contrast to
\mathcal{N}
, the duality defect
C\mathcal{N}
commutes with the right-moving chiral algebra. The corresponding twisted partition function is the parity-flip
\tau\leftrightarrow\bar{\tau}
of (
3.24
). The same relation holds for other twisted partition functions discussed here and in the following sections.

We have used the fact that the right-moving
U(1)_{2k}
vacuum character twisted by
\mathcal{N}
gives,

\displaystyle K_{0}^{k}(\bar{\tau})\xlongrightarrow{\mathcal{N}}\sum_{n\in{\mathbb{Z}}}(-1)^{m}\chi_{m^{2}}(\bar{\tau})\,,

(3.25)

and also the following relation for the degenerate Virasoro characters

\displaystyle\sum_{m\in{\mathbb{Z}}_{\geq 0}}(-1)^{m}\chi_{m^{2}}=\sum_{m\in{\mathbb{Z}}}{(-1)^{m}q^{m^{2}}\over\eta}\,.

(3.26)

One can immediately check that the twisted partition function is compatible with modular invariance and integrality.
Indeed the modular S-transform gives

\displaystyle Z_{\mathcal{N}1}(\tau,\bar{\tau})=

\displaystyle{\displaystyle 1\over 2|\eta(\tau)|^{2}}\sum_{n,m\in{\mathbb{Z}}}q^{n^{2}\over 4k}\bar{q}^{(m+1/2)^{2}\over 4}=\sum_{n=-k+1}^{k}\sum_{m\in{\mathbb{Z}}_{\geq 0}}K_{n}^{k}(\tau)\chi_{(m+1/2)^{2}\over 4}(\bar{\tau})\,,

(3.27)

which decomposes into
U(1)_{2k}
and Virasoro characters with integral degeneracies, as required.

For odd
k
, a similar analysis gives the
\mathcal{N}
twisted torus partition function

\displaystyle Z_{1\mathcal{N}}(\tau,\bar{\tau})=

\displaystyle{\displaystyle\sqrt{k}\over|\eta(\tau)|^{2}}\left(\sum_{n\in{\mathbb{Z}}}(-1)^{n}q^{kn^{2}}\right)\left(\sum_{m\in{\mathbb{Z}}}(-1)^{m}\bar{q}^{m^{2}}\right)={\sqrt{k}\theta_{4}(2k\tau)\theta_{4}(2\bar{\tau})\over|\eta(\tau)|^{2}}\,,

(3.28)

where we have used the fact that the
U(1)_{2k}
vacuum character twisted by
\mathcal{N}
gives,

\displaystyle K_{0}^{k}(\tau)\xlongrightarrow{\mathcal{N}}{1\over\eta(\tau)}\sum_{n\in{\mathbb{Z}}}(-1)^{n}q^{kn^{2}}\,.

(3.29)

Performing an S-transform, we obtain

\displaystyle Z_{\mathcal{N}1}(\tau,\bar{\tau})=

\displaystyle{\displaystyle 1\over 2|\eta(\tau)|^{2}}\sum_{n,m\in{\mathbb{Z}}}q^{(n+1/2)^{2}\over 4k}\bar{q}^{(m+1/2)^{2}\over 4}=2\sum_{n,m\in{\mathbb{Z}}_{\geq 0}}\chi_{(n+1/2)^{2}\over 4k}(\tau)\chi_{(m+1/2)^{2}\over 4}(\bar{\tau})\,.

(3.30)

Note that for
k=1
, the duality defect becomes invertible and generates a non-anomalous
{\mathbb{Z}}_{2}
T-duality symmetry (
3.8
). The twisted partition function and its S-transform for this case are

\displaystyle Z_{1\mathcal{N}}(\tau,\bar{\tau})={\theta_{4}(2\tau)\theta_{4}(2\bar{\tau})\over|\eta(\tau)|^{2}}\,,\quad Z_{\mathcal{N}1}(\tau,\bar{\tau})={\theta_{2}(\tau/2)\theta_{2}(\bar{\tau}/2)\over 2|\eta(\tau)|^{2}}\,.

(3.31)

3.3.3
Spin selection rules

Here we derive relations between the spectrum of spins in the defect Hilbert space
\mathcal{H}_{\mathcal{N}}
for the duality TDL and the F-symbols of the TY category. As we will see, they lead to further constraints on the F-symbol data of
{\mathbb{Z}}_{k}
TY symmetries, namely the primitive
k
-th root of unity
\omega
and FS indicator
\epsilon
.

Figure 9:
To define the
\mathbb{Z}_{k}
symmetry action in the
\mathcal{N}
-twisted sector, we must choose a resolution of the crossings into 3-fold junctions. We refer to the resolution depicted in the figure as the “minus” resolution, and the operators associated with group elements
g=h=r^{a}\in\mathbb{Z}_{k}
as
\widehat{(r^{a})}_{-}
. This is related to the other resolution by a phase
\widehat{(r^{a})}_{+}=\omega^{a^{2}}\widehat{(r^{a})}_{-}
.

We define the action of the
{\mathbb{Z}}_{k}
symmetry TDL
\mathcal{L}
on the defect Hilbert space
\mathcal{H}_{\mathcal{N}}
as
\hat{\mathcal{L}}_{-}
as in Figure
9
. The bicharacter
e^{i\chi(a,b)}=\omega^{ab}
enters in the crossing relations involving both the duality TDL
\mathcal{N}
and the
{\mathbb{Z}}_{k}
generators
r^{a}
and
r^{b}
. In particular,

\displaystyle\widehat{(r^{a})}_{-}\cdot\widehat{(r^{b})}_{-}=\omega^{ab}\widehat{(r^{a+b})}_{-}\,,

(3.32)

which implies

\displaystyle\widehat{(r^{a})}_{-}=\omega^{-{a(a-1)\over 2}}(\widehat{r}_{-})^{a}\,,

(3.33)

and

(\widehat{r}_{-})^{k}=\omega^{k(k-1)\over 2}=(-1)^{k-1}\,.

(3.34)

We would like to derive a relation between the spin spectrum in
\mathcal{H}_{\mathcal{N}}
and the choice of bicharacter (
3.15
). We proceed as in
[
12
]
to consider the cylinder amplitude between a pair of states
|\psi\rangle,|\psi^{\prime}\rangle\in\mathcal{H}_{\mathcal{N}}
of equal conformal weights

\displaystyle\langle\psi^{\prime}|q^{L_{0}-{c\over 24}}\bar{q}^{\bar{L}_{0}-{\bar{c}\over 24}}|\psi\rangle\,.

(3.35)

\displaystyle~~\longrightarrow^{\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!T^{2}}

\displaystyle~={\epsilon\over\sqrt{k}}\sum_{a=0}^{k-1}

Figure 10:
Applying partial fusion and crossing to the
T^{2}
transformation of
{\cal N}
line in time direction.

By performing a
T^{2}
transformation
\tau\to\tau+2
on this configuration (which involves the duality
\mathcal{N}
in the time direction), and applying partial fusion and crossing relations, as in Figure
10
, we reach the equation

\displaystyle e^{4\pi is}\langle\psi^{\prime}|\psi\rangle={\epsilon\over\sqrt{k}}\langle\psi^{\prime}|\sum_{a=0}^{k-1}\widehat{(r^{a})}_{-}|\psi\rangle\,.

(3.36)

Let us restrict our attention to
{\mathbb{Z}}_{k}
eigenstates in
\mathcal{H}_{\mathcal{N}}
. Since
\omega
is a primitive
k
-th root of unity, these states satisfy

\displaystyle\hat{r}_{-}|\psi\rangle=\omega^{q}|\psi\rangle\,,

(3.37)

with
q\in{\mathbb{Z}}
for
k
odd and
q\in{\mathbb{Z}}+{1\over 2}
for
k
even (cf. (
3.34
)). Then (
3.36
) can be rewritten as

\displaystyle e^{4\pi is}={\epsilon\over\sqrt{k}}\sum_{a=0}^{k-1}\bar{\omega}^{a(a-1)\over 2}\omega^{qa}={\epsilon\over\sqrt{k}}\sum_{a=0}^{k-1}\omega^{2qa-a(a-1)\over 2}={\epsilon\over\sqrt{k}}\sum_{a=0}^{k-1}\omega^{-{1\over 2}{(a-{2q+1\over 2})^{2}}}\omega^{(2q+1)^{2}\over 8}\,.

(3.38)

For
k
odd, using the periodicity in
a\to a+k
, we have

\displaystyle e^{4\pi is}={\epsilon\over\sqrt{k}}\omega^{(2q+1)^{2}\over 8}\sum_{a=0}^{k-1}\omega^{-{(a-1/2)^{2}\over 2}}\,.

(3.39)

Similarly for
k
even, using the periodicity in
a\to a+k
, redefining
p=q+{1\over 2}\in{\mathbb{Z}}_{k}
16
16

16

Here the fractional powers of
\omega
are taken in the standard branch. Consequently for fixed
p\in{\mathbb{Z}}_{k}
, the RHS of (
3.40
) in general changes as
\omega\to we^{2\pi i}
. However the set of
e^{4\pi is}
for
p\in{\mathbb{Z}}_{k}
does not depend on such a change of branch (it can be canceled by shifting
p\to p+{k\over 2}
).

\displaystyle e^{4\pi is}={\epsilon\over\sqrt{k}}\sum_{a=0}^{k-1}\omega^{-{(a-p)^{2}\over 2}}\omega^{p^{2}\over 2}={\epsilon\over\sqrt{k}}\omega^{p^{2}\over 2}\sum_{a=0}^{k-1}\omega^{-{a^{2}\over 2}}\,.

(3.40)

These Gauss sums constrain the allowed spins in the defect Hilbert space
\mathcal{H}_{\mathcal{N}}
in terms of the F-symbol data of the TY category (
\epsilon
and
\omega
here) and vice versa.

The second Gauss sum (
3.40
) may be found in Appendix 4 of
[
65
]
, where it is shown that

\frac{1}{\sqrt{k}}\sum_{a=0}^{k-1}\omega^{-{a^{2}\over 2}}=e^{-{2\pi i\sigma\over 8}},

(3.41)

and
\sigma
is the signature of the associated quadratic form on
{\mathbb{Z}}_{k}

q(a)=\omega^{a^{2}\over 2}.

(3.42)

Namely, we must find an (even) integer bilinear form
\langle-,-\rangle
on some
\mathbb{Z}^{n}
lattice with discriminant group
\mathbb{Z}_{k}
and associated quadratic form
q
, and then
\sigma
is the signature of
\langle-,-\rangle
. There is an algorithm for producing such a lift by Wall
[
66
]
. This lift is not unique and can only define a signature modulo 8 because of the existence of even bilinear forms with nonzero signature and trivial discriminant group, such as the
E_{8}
root lattice. See
[
67
]
for a discussion of these facts in another physics context. In particular, if
\omega=e^{\pm{2\pi i\over k}}
,
e^{2\pi i\sigma\over 8}=e^{\pm{\pi i\over 4}}
which comes from a rank one bilinear form
\langle x,y\rangle\equiv\pm kxy
for
x,y\in{\mathbb{Z}}
.

It turns out the first Gauss sum (
3.39
) is also related to a signature
[
68
]
. Namely, it is
17
17

17

Note that unlike the
k
even case, here the summand
q(a)\equiv\omega^{-{(a-1/2)^{2}\over 2}}
defines a quadratic function on
{\mathbb{Z}}_{k}\to U(1)
but not a quadratic form on
{\mathbb{Z}}_{k}
(i.e.
q(na)\neq q(a)^{n^{2}}
).

\frac{1}{\sqrt{k}}\sum_{a=0}^{k-1}\omega^{-{(a-1/2)^{2}\over 2}}=e^{-{2\pi i\sigma\over 8}},

(3.43)

where now we must find an integer bilinear form
\langle-,-\rangle
with discriminant group
{\mathbb{Z}}_{k}
associated to the bilinear form
\omega^{ab}
, and a Wu (characteristic) element
w\in{\mathbb{R}}^{n}
such that

\langle x,w\rangle=\langle x,x\rangle\mod 2\qquad\forall x\in\mathbb{Z}^{n}\,,

(3.44)

and such that
w
lifts the generator of the discriminant group
1\in\mathbb{Z}_{k}
.
In particular, if
\omega=e^{\pm{2\pi i\over k}}
, we can take a rank one lattice with bilinear form
\langle x,y\rangle=\pm kxy
and Wu element
w=\pm{1\over k}
. Consequently we find
e^{2\pi i\sigma\over 8}=e^{\pm{\pi i\over 4}}
and again the signature
\sigma
has a mod 8 ambiguity due to the
E_{8}
lattice.

To summarize, the spin selection rules we have derived for
\mathcal{H}_{\mathcal{N}}
with bicharacter
\chi_{\pm}
and general FS indicator
\epsilon
are,

\displaystyle e^{4\pi is}=\begin{cases}\epsilon e^{\pm{\pi i\over 4k}(2n+1)^{2}}e^{\mp\pi i/4}&k\in 2{\mathbb{Z}}\,,\\
\epsilon e^{\pm{\pi i\over k}n^{2}}e^{\mp\pi i/4}&k\in 2{\mathbb{Z}}+1\,.\end{cases}

(3.45)

Let us now apply the above spin selection rules to infer about the F-symbols and determine which TY category symmetry is present in the
U(1)_{2k}
CFT. From (
3.30
) and (
3.27
), one can read off the spins in
\mathcal{H}_{\mathcal{N}}
,

\displaystyle s+{1\over 16}=\begin{cases}{(2m+1)^{2}\over 16k}+{1\over 2}{\mathbb{Z}}&k\in 2{\mathbb{Z}}+1\,,\\
{n^{2}\over 4k}+{1\over 2}{\mathbb{Z}}&k\in 2{\mathbb{Z}}\,,\end{cases}

(3.46)

where
m,n\in{\mathbb{Z}}
.

For
k
odd, it is immediate to check that out of the four possibilities given by
\omega=e^{\pm{2\pi i\over k}}
and
\epsilon=\pm 1
for the TY symmetry, only the case
\omega=e^{{2\pi i\over k}}
and
\epsilon=1
produces the spin selection rule (
3.39
) which is consistent with the above spin spectrum. This completes the identification of the TY category symmetry on the circle branch (
3.17
) for the
k
odd case.

For
k
even, while the spin selection rule (
3.40
) with
\omega=e^{{2\pi i\over k}}
and
\epsilon=1
is satisfied by the above spin spectrum (
3.46
), other solutions to this constraint are possible at special values of
k
. To eliminate these spurious possibilities, we carry out a bootstrap analysis that utilizes the left-moving
U(1)_{2k}
chiral algebra which commutes with the duality for even
k
.

3.3.4
Bootstrap analysis for
k
even

The idea here is to analyze the modular bootstrap equation (
2.5
) for the torus partition function of the
U(1)_{2k}
CFT decorated by the
{\mathbb{Z}}_{k}
symmetry TDL
r^{a}
and the putative duality TDL
\mathcal{N}
, to derive constraints on the
{\mathbb{Z}}_{k}
charge of the states in the twisted Hilbert space
\mathcal{H}_{\mathcal{N}}
(e.g.
q
in (
3.37
)). As we will see, this gives a refinement of the spin selection rule derived in the last section and determines the F-symbols for
\mathcal{N}
completely to be those in (
3.17
).

We start with the torus partition function with a
{\mathbb{Z}}_{k}
TDL along the spatial cycle,

\displaystyle Z_{1r}(\tau,\bar{\tau})=\sum_{m=-k+1}^{k}e^{2\pi im\over k}|K_{m}^{k}(\tau)|^{2}\,.

(3.47)

Its modular S-transform captures the states in the defect Hilbert space
\mathcal{H}_{r}
,

\displaystyle Z_{r1}(\tau,\bar{\tau})=\sum_{n=0}^{2k-1}K_{n}^{k}(\tau)K_{n-2}^{k}(\bar{\tau})\,.

(3.48)

If we insert a duality TDL along the spatial cycle, the corresponding partition function is the trace of
\widehat{\mathcal{N}}
in
\mathcal{H}_{r}
. Using F-moves with the F-symbols parametrized by the undetermined parameters
\omega=e^{\pm{2\pi i\over k}}
and
\epsilon=\pm 1
as in Figure
11
, we obtain

\displaystyle\widehat{\mathcal{N}}^{2}=\bar{\omega}\sum_{n=0}^{k-1}\bar{\omega}^{n}(\widehat{r})^{n}\,.

(3.49)

\displaystyle\left.\widehat{\mathcal{N}}^{2}\right|_{\mathcal{H}_{r^{m}}}=

\displaystyle={\epsilon\over\sqrt{k}}\sum_{n=0}^{k-1}

\displaystyle={\epsilon\over\sqrt{k}}\sum_{n=0}^{k-1}\bar{\omega}^{m(m+n)}

\displaystyle={\epsilon\over\sqrt{k}}\sum_{n=0}^{k-1}\bar{\omega}^{m(m+n)}

\displaystyle=\sum_{n=0}^{k-1}\bar{\omega}^{m(m+n)}

Figure 11:
The sequence of F-moves that simplifies the squared action of the duality TDL
\widehat{\mathcal{N}}^{2}
on the defect Hilbert space
\mathcal{H}_{r^{m}}
.

This means that acting on
\mathcal{H}_{r}
,
\widehat{\mathcal{N}}
annihilates a state unless it has
{\mathbb{Z}}_{k}
charge 1 if
\omega=e^{2\pi i\over k}
and charge
-1
if
\omega=e^{-{2\pi i\over k}}
. Note that each term
K_{n}^{k}(\tau)K^{k}_{n-2}(\bar{\tau})
in (
3.48
) carries
{\mathbb{Z}}_{k}
charge
n-1
. Moreover since the duality acts as an automorphism of the right-moving
U(1)_{2k}
chiral algebra, consistency with OPE requires that (non-local) right-moving primary operators
e^{i{n\over\sqrt{2k}}X_{R}}
(from the block
K_{n}^{k}(\bar{\tau})
) are mapped to
e^{-i{n\over\sqrt{2k}}X_{R}}
under the duality action
\widehat{\mathcal{N}}
. Consequently, the trace of
\widehat{\mathcal{N}}
can only receive contributions from the right moving vacuum block
K_{0}^{k}(\bar{\tau})
, which transforms according to (
3.29
). Now
\mathcal{H}_{r}
does not contain states with
{\mathbb{Z}}_{k}
charge
-1
if the right-moving part resides in
K_{0}^{k}(\bar{\tau})
which requires
n=2
or
n=k-2
in (
3.48
). As a result, we find

\displaystyle Z_{r\mathcal{N}}(\tau,\bar{\tau})=\begin{cases}{\gamma}e^{-{\pi i\over k}}\sum_{m\in{\mathbb{Z}}_{\geq 0}}K_{2}^{k}(\tau)(-1)^{m}\chi_{m^{2}}(\bar{\tau})&\omega=e^{2\pi i\over k}\,,\\
0&\omega=e^{-{2\pi i\over k}}\,,\end{cases}

(3.50)

with
{\gamma}=\pm 1
.

The modular S-dual transform gives the partition function
Z_{\mathcal{N}r}
which computes the trace of the TDL
\hat{r}_{-}
acting on the defect Hilbert space
\mathcal{H}_{\mathcal{N}}
. The defect ground state with weight
(h,\bar{h})=(0,{1\over 16})
is non-degenerate (
3.27
). Since
\hat{r}_{-}
is invertible, this implies that
Z_{\mathcal{N}r}
cannot vanish identically. Therefore we can conclude that only the first solution in (
3.50
) is possible and
\omega=e^{2\pi i\over k}
as desired.

Next we want to derive the Frobenius-Schur indicator
\epsilon=1
. Along the way we will see that
\gamma=1
as required by the bootstrap equation and the spin selection rule. Upon S-transform, we have

\displaystyle Z_{\mathcal{N}r_{-}}(\tau,\bar{\tau})=

\displaystyle{\displaystyle\gamma}e^{-{\pi i\over k}}\sum_{n=-k+1}^{k}\sum_{m\in{\mathbb{Z}}_{\geq 0}}e^{2\pi in\over k}K_{n}^{k}(\tau)\chi^{(m+1/2)^{2}\over 4}(\bar{\tau})\,.

(3.51)

From the above partition function, we see the defect ground state
|\psi_{0}\rangle
with spin
s=-{1\over 16}
carries charge

\displaystyle\widehat{r}_{-}|\psi_{0}\rangle={\gamma}e^{-{\pi i\over k}}|\psi_{0}\rangle\,.

(3.52)

The doubly-degenerate states
|\psi_{\pm 1}\rangle
of weight
(h,\bar{h})=({1\over 4k},{1\over 16})
have charges

\displaystyle\widehat{r}_{-}|\psi_{\pm 1}\rangle={\gamma}e^{-{\pi i\over k}}e^{\pm{2\pi i\over k}}|\psi_{\pm 1}\rangle\,.

(3.53)

The spin selection rule (
3.40
) applied to these three states reads

\displaystyle\epsilon=e^{{\pi ip^{2}\over k}}\,,\quad e^{\pi i\over k}\epsilon=e^{{\pi i(p+1)^{2}\over k}}=e^{{\pi i(p-1)^{2}\over k}}\,,

(3.54)

with
e^{{2\pi ip\over k}}={\gamma}
. It then immediately follows that
{\gamma}=1
and
\epsilon=1
. This completes the determination of the TY fusion category symmetries in (
3.17
).
18
18

18

Note that the parameter
{\alpha}
for the duality defect does not change the analysis here for both
k
even and odd. This is because the duality twisted partition function
Z_{1\mathcal{N}}
is independent of
{\alpha}
(see around (
3.24
)). The analysis for the duality defect
C\mathcal{N}
(and its
{\alpha}
-family) is also completely parallel which involves a parity-flip of the twisted partition functions for
\mathcal{N}
(see Footnote
15
) and leads to a conjugate bicharacter
\chi(a,b)=-{2\pi iab\over k}
but the same FS indicator
\epsilon=1
.

3.3.5
Special cases

As a very simple example, taking
k=2
we find that the special radius where
2R={2\over R}
is the Dirac radius
R=1
. As explained in Section
2.6
, the self-duality of this (bosonic) theory under
\mathbb{Z}_{2}
gauging arises from the chiral fermion parity of a single Majorana factor of the Dirac fermion (which can be thought of as two Majorana fermions with the diagonal GSO projection), just as the Kramers-Wannier duality does for the critical Ising model
[
42
,
44
]
(see Table
2
). See also
[
43
]
.

Another important case is
k=4
, as this radius
R=\sqrt{8}
describes the Kosterlitz-Thouless (KT) point where the circle branch meets the
\mathbb{Z}_{2}
orbifold branch. We will see in Section
4.2
that that entire orbifold branch has
\mathbb{Z}_{4}
TY symmetries. This can be related to a chiral parity symmetry of
\mathbb{Z}_{4}
parafermions, which we explain in Section
4.4
.

3.4
Symmetric RG flows from the circle branch

The
{\mathbb{Z}}_{k}
TY symmetries are all anomalous
[
14
]
(the corresponding TY categories do not admit fiber functors
[
69
,
14
]
). Consequently any RG flow respecting such symmetries cannot end at a trivially gapped phase in the IR. Below we discuss the implications for RG flows from CFTs on the circle branch preserving such symmetries.

In the case of the
U(1)_{2k}
CFT on the circle branch, the theory admits relevant scalar operators commuting with the
{\rm TY}({\mathbb{Z}}_{k},\chi_{\pm},1)
symmetry (with
{\alpha}=0
and the
+
sign in (
3.17
)) for
k=2,3
, given by linear combinations of

\displaystyle k=2:\quad V_{2,0}+V_{0,1}\,,\quad V_{-2,0}+V_{0,-1}\,,\quad h=\bar{h}={1\over 2}\,,

(3.55)

\displaystyle k=3:\quad V_{3,0}+V_{0,1}\,,\quad V_{-3,0}+V_{0,-1}\,,\quad h=\bar{h}={3\over 4}\,,

and marginal deformations for
k=4

\displaystyle k=4:\quad V_{4,0}+V_{0,1}\,,\quad V_{-4,0}+V_{0,-1}\,,\quad h=\bar{h}=1\,.

(3.56)

Such deformations preserving the TY symmetry become irrelevant for
k\geq 5
.

For simplicity let us consider the linear combination of the above deformations that preserve the
{\mathbb{Z}}_{2}^{C}
symmetry of the circle CFT as well. The anomalous fusion category symmetry requires the deformed theory to be a nontrivial CFT (or TQFT) in the IR which realizes the same symmetry or the symmetry must be spontaneously broken. Below we will see explicitly these deformed
c=1
CFTs indeed retain the TY symmetries by flowing to a gapless phase.

For
k=4
, the CFT is at the Kosterlitz-Thouless point, which is the intersection of the circle and orbifold branches of the
c=1
moduli space. The
{\mathbb{Z}}_{2}^{C}
symmetric combination of (
3.56
) leads to precisely the exactly marginal deformation that moves onto the orbifold branch
[
70
]
. Indeed we will show in Section
4.2
that the
{\rm TY}({\mathbb{Z}}_{4},\chi_{\pm},1)
symmetries persist on the entire orbifold branch.

The
c=1
CFT at
k=2
is the diagonal bosonization of the Dirac fermion. From the bosonization map, it is easy to see that the
{\mathbb{Z}}_{2}^{C}
symmetric relevant deformation
V_{2,0}+V_{0,1}+V_{-2,0}+V_{0,-1}
corresponds to the mass term for one of the two Majorana fermions. Consequently the theory flows to the Ising CFT in the IR, with the
{\mathbb{Z}}_{2}
TY symmetry acting as Kramers-Wannier duality.

The RCFT at
k=3
is described by the
SU(2)^{D_{4}}_{4}/U(1)
coset with a non-diagonal modular invariant of the
D_{4}
type
[
71
]
. This is related to the
SU(2)^{A_{5}}_{4}/U(1)
coset CFT with diagonal modular invariant of the
A_{5}
type by gauging the
{\mathbb{Z}}_{2}
charge conjugation symmetry in (
B.6
). Both CFTs come from two distinct bosonizations of the
{\mathbb{Z}}_{4}
parafermions in
[
56
]
(see also Appendix
B
for this and a review of parafermions). As shown in
[
72
]
, the
SU(2)^{A_{5}}_{4}/U(1)
coset CFT admits an integrable RG flow to the tetracritical Ising model triggered by the parafermion bilinear deformation
e^{\pi i\over 4}\psi_{1}\tilde{\psi}_{1}+e^{-{\pi i\over 4}}\psi_{3}\tilde{\psi}_{3}
of weight
h=\bar{h}={3\over 4}
, which is
{\mathbb{Z}}_{2}
symmetric.
19
19

19

The general statement in
[
72
]
involves an integrable RG flow from the
SU(2)^{A_{k+1}}_{k}/U(1)
coset CFT deformed by
e^{\pi i\over k}\psi_{1}\tilde{\psi}_{1}+(c.c)
of weight
h=\bar{h}={k-1\over k}
to the
(A_{k},A_{k+1})
minimal model for
k\geq 3
.
Moreover, this operator corresponds to the charge conjugation invariant combination of the operators in (
3.55
) for
k=3
(since it’s the unique Virasoro primary with these weights that is
{\mathbb{Z}}_{2}
symmetric), as summarized in the diagram below,

\displaystyle\hbox to319.04pt{\vbox to53.52pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\hskip 159.51718pt\lower-26.22005pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {}{}{}{{}}\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\hbox{\hbox{{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {{}{}{{{}}{{}}{{}}{{}}}{{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}}{{}{}{{

(3.57)

Gauging the
{\mathbb{Z}}_{2}
symmetry along the entire RG flow, this implies that the
SU(2)^{D_{4}}_{4}/U(1)
coset CFT flows, under the symmetric relevant deformation in (
3.55
), to the three-state Potts model which is the
{\mathbb{Z}}_{2}
orbifold of the tetracritical Ising model. The three-state Potts is equivalently described by the
SU(2)_{3}^{A_{4}}/U(1)
coset CFT, which evidently hosts the
{\rm TY}({\mathbb{Z}}_{3},\chi_{\pm},1)
fusion category symmetries following the general discussions in Section
2.6
.

3.5
Continuum of Topological Defects from
SU(2)_{1}

At the self-dual radius
R=\sqrt{2}
, the
(U(1)^{\theta}\times U(1)^{\phi})\rtimes\mathbb{Z}_{2}^{C}
symmetry of the compact boson is enhanced to
SO(4)
. When gauging the
\mathbb{Z}_{n}^{\phi}
subgroup of
U(1)^{\phi}
, which takes us to the radius
R=n\sqrt{2}
, those
SO(4)
rotations which do not commute with
\mathbb{Z}_{n}^{\phi}
define TDLs of quantum dimension
n
according to the method of Section
2.5
.

There are four defect currents associated with these TDLs, given by vertex operators with momentum and winding
(p,w)=(\pm n,\pm 1/n)
. Because of the fractional winding, these currents are not local, but sit at the end of
\mathbb{Z}_{n}^{\theta}
TDLs, which generate the magnetic symmetry of the orbifold. By then gauging a
\mathbb{Z}_{m}^{\theta}
symmetry, with
m
coprime to
n
, we obtain a continuum family at radius
R=n\sqrt{2}/m
, with defect currents given by
(p,w)=(\pm n/m,\pm m/n)
.

This family of TDLs was constructed previously in
[
73
]
, whose authors argued that they form a complete set of TDLs for the compact boson at these radii. For example, this family contains the
\mathbb{Z}_{k}
TY TDLs discussed in Section
3.3
for
k=n^{2}
. To see this consider the
SO(4)
element

\begin{bmatrix}0&&I_{2\times 2}\\
I_{2\times 2}&&0\end{bmatrix}\,,

(3.58)

which describes the action of T-duality in (
3.8
). At the self-dual point, this acts as a symmetry
V_{p,w}\mapsto(-1)^{pw}V_{w,p}
, with the sign coming from the relation
V_{p,w}={:}V_{p,0}V_{0,w}{:}=(-1)^{pw}{:}V_{0,w}V_{p,0}{:}
. We will use
V_{p,w}^{\prime}
for the moment to denote the vertex operators at
R=n\sqrt{2}
. These operators come from
V_{p/n,nw}
at the self-dual point when gauging the
\mathbb{Z}_{n}^{\phi}
symmetry therein. For
p\in n\mathbb{Z}
we thus obtain the action on these operators using

V^{\prime}_{p,w}\sim V_{p/n,nw}\mapsto n(-1)^{pw}V_{nw,p/n}\sim n(-1)^{pw}V^{\prime}_{n^{2}w,p/n^{2}}.

(3.59)

The factor of
n
comes from the sum over
\mathbb{Z}_{n}^{\phi}
orbit in (
2.19
). Thus we recover the action on vertex operators in (
3.21
).

4
Fusion Categories of the
\mathbb{Z}_{2}
Orbifold

In this section, we study fusion category symmetries on the orbifold branch of the
c=1
CFT which persist to all values of the marginal parameter. We will follow a similar strategy as in the previous section. In particular, the
\mathbb{Z}_{2}
orbifold has a global symmetry group
D_{8}
and is self-dual under gauging various subgroups. This leads to two
\mathbb{Z}_{4}
TY categories TY
(\mathbb{Z}_{4},\chi_{\pm},+)
(Section
4.2
) and two
D_{4}=\mathbb{Z}_{2}^{2}
TY categories,
{\rm Rep}(D_{8})
and
{\rm Rep}(H_{8})
(Section
4.3
). We then discuss these symmetries at rational points on the orbifold branch where they can be understood using Verlinde lines and ordinary symmetries in (para)fermion theories. Furthermore we argue that these fusion categories are part of a continuum of TDLs which exists at all
R
and are enhanced at special values
R=n\sqrt{2}
.

4.1
Operator Content of the
\mathbb{Z}_{2}
Orbifold

The
c=1
orbifold at radius
R
(“the
\mathbb{Z}_{2}
orbifold”) is obtained by gauging the
{\mathbb{Z}}_{2}^{C}
charge conjugation symmetry of the compact boson of the same radius. In terms of the (normalized) non-chiral compact boson
\theta\equiv{X_{L}+X_{R}\over R}
and its T-dual
\phi\equiv{R(X_{L}-X_{R})\over 2}
(normalized such that both
\theta
and
\phi
have unit radii)

\displaystyle{\mathbb{Z}}_{2}^{C}:(\theta,\phi)\to(-\theta,-\phi)\,.

(4.1)

The spectrum of Virasoro primaries on the
c=1
orbifold branch consists of two sectors, the untwisted sector and the
{\mathbb{Z}}_{2}
twisted sector. The latter is charged under the magnetic symmetry
{\mathbb{Z}}_{2}^{M}
.

The twisted sector consists of two primary operators

\displaystyle\sigma_{1}{\rm~and~}\sigma_{2},\quad(h,\bar{h})=\left({1\over 16},{1\over 16}\right)

(4.2)

that correspond to the ground states at the two fixed points of
S^{1}/{\mathbb{Z}}_{2}^{C}
, as well the first excited states

\displaystyle\tau_{1}{\rm~and~}\tau_{2},\quad(h,\bar{h})=\left({9\over 16},{9\over 16}\right).

(4.3)

The untwisted sector includes reflection-invariant momentum-winding operators
V^{+}_{n,w}
defined by

\displaystyle V^{+}_{n,w}={V_{n,w}+V_{-n,-w}\over\sqrt{2}}\,,

(4.4)

where
V_{n,w}
denotes the usual momentum-winding operators in the unorbifolded theory (
3.4
).
The rest of the Virasoro primaries in the untwisted sector are built from
{\mathbb{Z}}_{2}^{C}
invariant normal-ordered Schur polynomials in the
U(1)
currents
d\theta,d\phi
and their derivatives in the unorbifolded theory
[
27
]
,

\displaystyle j_{n^{2}}j_{m^{2}}~{\rm with}~{m-n\in 2{\mathbb{Z}}}\,,~(h,\bar{h})=(n^{2},m^{2})\,.

(4.5)

For example,

\displaystyle j_{1}=\partial X_{L}\,,\quad j_{4}={:}j_{1}^{4}{:}-2{:}j_{1}\partial^{2}j_{1}{:}+{3\over 2}{:}(\partial j_{1})^{2}{:}\,.

(4.6)

The exactly marginal operator that gives rise to the orbifold branch is
j_{1}\bar{j}_{1}
. The spin-one
U(1)
currents themselves are projected out in the orbifold, but there is a spin
4n^{2}
left-moving (and right-moving) current for every
n\in{\mathbb{Z}}_{+}
at general
R
and they generate a W-algebra of type
W(2,4)
that extends the Virasoro algebra. At special radii, this chiral algebra can be further enhanced to an even larger W-algebra such that the CFT becomes rational, with a finite number of primaries and conformal blocks for this chiral algebra.

The orbifold branch has global symmetry

\displaystyle G_{\rm orb}=D_{8}\,,

(4.7)

at generic
R
, which does not harbor ’t Hooft anomalies. However we will see the orbifold branch hosts a rich zoo of non-invertible TDLs that include TY categories associated to
{\mathbb{Z}}_{4}
and
D_{4}={\mathbb{Z}}_{2}\times{\mathbb{Z}}_{2}
subgroups of
D_{8}
. We adopt the following convention for
D_{8}

\displaystyle D_{8}=\langle s,r|s^{2}=r^{4}=(rs)^{2}=1\rangle\,.

(4.8)

In the 2
\times
2 Pauli-matix representation of
D_{8}
, we have
s=\sigma^{x}
,
sr=\sigma^{z}
,
r=\sigma^{x}\sigma^{z}
,
r^{2}=-1
, etc.

The appearance of
D_{8}
symmetry can be understood from the
{\mathbb{Z}}_{2}^{C}
discrete gauging as follows. Recall the unorbifolded boson at generic
R
has global symmetry

\displaystyle G=(U(1)^{\theta}\times U(1)^{\phi})\rtimes{\mathbb{Z}}_{2}^{C}\,,

(4.9)

where
{\mathbb{Z}}_{2}^{C}
negates the parameters of both
U(1)
’s. The commutant of
{\mathbb{Z}}_{2}^{C}
in
G/{\mathbb{Z}}_{2}^{C}
is
{\mathbb{Z}}_{2}^{\theta}\times{\mathbb{Z}}_{2}^{\phi}
generated by
\pi
-translations in
\theta
or
\phi
. These symmetries continue to act as global symmetries in the orbifolded theory in both the twisted and untwisted sectors. In addition, we have the magnetic symmetry
{\mathbb{Z}}_{2}^{M}
which only acts non-trivially in the twisted sector, where it acts as a scalar
-1
. Due to a mixed anomaly between
\mathbb{Z}_{2}^{C}
and its commutant, we arrive at the group extension

\displaystyle 1\to{\mathbb{Z}}_{2}^{M}\to D_{8}\to{\mathbb{Z}}_{2}^{\theta}\times{\mathbb{Z}}_{2}^{\phi}\to 1\,.

(4.10)

See Appendix
A.2
for more details. In particular, we find
{\mathbb{Z}}_{2}^{M}
is identified with the center of
D_{8}
, generated by
r^{2}
, and
D_{8}
acts on the untwisted sector through its quotient
{\mathbb{Z}}_{2}^{\theta}\times{\mathbb{Z}}_{2}^{\phi}
,

\displaystyle r:(\theta,\phi)\to(\theta+\pi,\phi+\pi)\,,~s:(\theta,\phi)\to(\theta,\phi+\pi)\,.

(4.11)

For the operators in the twisted sector,
D_{8}
acts as

\displaystyle r:(\sigma_{1},\sigma_{2})\to(i\sigma_{1},-i\sigma_{2})\,,~(\tau_{1},\tau_{2})\to(i\tau_{1},-i\tau_{2})\,,

(4.12)

\displaystyle s:(\sigma_{1},\sigma_{2})\to(\sigma_{2},\sigma_{1})\,,~(\tau_{1},\tau_{2})\to(\tau_{2},\tau_{1})\,.

In Appendix
A.2
, we show that this
D_{8}
symmetry is anomaly-free, despite its chiral-looking action (
4.11
).

The
D_{8}
symmetry has the following important subgroups

\langle r\rangle=\mathbb{Z}_{4}^{r}\,,\quad\langle sr^{3}\rangle=\mathbb{Z}_{2}^{\eta}\,,\quad\langle sr\rangle=\mathbb{Z}_{2}^{\sigma}\,,

(4.13)

where the superscript indicates the name of the TDL generating the symmetry, and

\displaystyle\langle r^{2},s\rangle=D_{4}^{A}\,,\quad\langle r^{2},sr\rangle=D_{4}^{B}\,,

(4.14)

where
D_{4}=\mathbb{Z}_{2}\times\mathbb{Z}_{2}
.

Along the orbifold branch, the RCFTs locate at radius
R=\sqrt{2k}
for
k\in{\mathbb{Z}}

[
74
]
. The enhanced chiral algebra is generated by

\displaystyle T\,,~j_{4}\,,~\cos\sqrt{2k}X_{L}\,,

(4.15)

of spin
2,4,k
respectively. There are
k+7
chiral primaries listed in Table
6
.

Primary

1

j

\phi^{i}_{k}

\phi_{m}

\sigma_{i}

\tau_{i}

h=\bar{h}

Table 6:
The
k+7
chiral primaries in the orbifold CFT
U(1)_{2k}/{\mathbb{Z}}_{2}
. Here
i=1,2
and
m=1,2,\dots,k-1
.

Here in writing down the characters in Table
6
we have introduced
[
75
]

\displaystyle W(\tau)\equiv{1\over\eta}\sum_{r\in{\mathbb{Z}}}(-1)^{r}q^{r^{2}}\,,\quad W(\tau)_{\pm}\equiv{1\over\eta}\sum_{r\in{\mathbb{Z}}}(\pm 1)^{r}q^{\left(r+{1\over 4}\right)^{2}}\,.

(4.16)

The partition function of the theory is given by the diagonal modular invariant

\displaystyle Z_{\rm orb}(R=\sqrt{2k})={1\over 2}Z_{\rm bos}(R=\sqrt{2k})+{1\over 2}|W|^{2}+|W_{+}|^{2}+|W_{-}|^{2}\,.

(4.17)

We will refer to such theories as the
U(1)_{2k}/{\mathbb{Z}}_{2}
CFTs.

4.2
\mathbb{Z}_{4}
Tambara-Yamagami Symmetry

Let us denote the orbifold partition function at radius
R
coupled to a
\mathbb{Z}_{4}
gauge field
A
on a closed spacetime
\Sigma
as
Z_{\rm orb}(R,\Sigma,A)
, where
\mathbb{Z}_{4}
is generated by the element
r\in D_{8}
(cf. (
4.8
)). This theory is self-dual under gauging
\mathbb{Z}_{4}
at any radius, meaning
20
20

20

Recall there is a similar identity for the partition function on the circle branch (
3.19
). As we will see this is not a coincidence. The
{\mathbb{Z}}_{4}
TY symmetry at the KT point on the circle branch can be continued to the orbifold branch since it preserves the corresponding marginal operator.

Z_{\rm orb}(R,\Sigma,A)=\frac{1}{\sqrt{|H^{1}(\Sigma,\mathbb{Z}_{4})|}}\sum_{B\in H^{1}(\Sigma,\mathbb{Z}_{4})}e^{\frac{\pm i\pi}{2}\int A\cup B}Z_{\rm orb}(R,\Sigma,B).

(4.18)

For an explicit check with
\Sigma=T^{2}
see Appendix
C
.

As in Section
3.3
, we expect that there is an associated
\mathbb{Z}_{4}
Tambara-Yamagami fusion category which acts on the theory. In general, there are four
{\mathbb{Z}}_{4}
TY categories labeled by the FS indicator
\epsilon=\pm
of the duality defect and a choice for the bicharacter

\displaystyle\chi_{\pm}(r^{a},r^{b})\equiv(\pm i)^{ab}\,,

(4.19)

both of which are consistent with (
4.18
).
21
21

21

None of the four TY categories admit fiber functors, and are thus anomalous according to
[
14
]
. Thus any CFT with such symmetries (including the orbifold CFTs here) cannot flow to trivially gapped phases under symmetric RG flows.

The
c=1
orbifold branch realizes the two
{\mathbb{Z}}_{4}
TY categories with
\epsilon=1
at general
R
and the two duality TDLs
\mathcal{N}_{{\mathbb{Z}}_{4}^{\pm}}
are related by stacking with the
{\mathbb{Z}}_{2}
symmetry defect associated to
s\in D_{8}
:

\displaystyle{\rm TY}({\mathbb{Z}}_{4},\chi_{+},+){\mathrel{\to}^{~~\mathcal{N}_{{\mathbb{Z}}_{4}^{\pm}}\ \mapsto\ s\mathcal{N}_{{\mathbb{Z}}_{4}^{\pm}}~~}}{\rm TY}({\mathbb{Z}}_{4},\chi_{-},+)\,.

(4.20)

We propose the following action on local primary operators

\displaystyle\widehat{\mathcal{N}}_{{\mathbb{Z}}_{4}^{+}}:

\displaystyle\begin{cases}V^{+}_{n,w}\to 2i^{n-w}V_{n,w}^{+}\quad{~\rm for~}n-w\in 2{\mathbb{Z}}\,,\\
j_{n^{2}}\bar{j}_{m^{2}}\to 2j_{n^{2}}\bar{j}_{m^{2}}\,,\\
\text{all other primaries}\to\text{non-local operators}\,,\end{cases}

(4.21)

\displaystyle\widehat{\mathcal{N}}_{{\mathbb{Z}}_{4}^{-}}:

\displaystyle\begin{cases}V^{+}_{n,w}\to 2i^{n+w}V_{n,w}^{+}\quad{~\rm for~}n-w\in 2{\mathbb{Z}}\,,\\
j_{n^{2}}\bar{j}_{m^{2}}\to 2j_{n^{2}}\bar{j}_{m^{2}}\,,\\
\text{all other primaries}\to\text{non-local operators}\,.\end{cases}

These actions have the following desired features. First, the duality defects annihilate operators that are charged under
{\mathbb{Z}}_{4}
and act on the
{\mathbb{Z}}_{4}
invariant operators with eigenvalue
\pm 2
as required by the TY fusion rule
\mathcal{N}^{2}=1+r+r^{2}+r^{3}
. They also solve the modular bootstrap equation (
2.5
) for torus with a single duality twist around the space or time cycle.

More explicitly,
the corresponding duality defects
\mathcal{N}_{{\mathbb{Z}}_{4}^{+}}
and
\mathcal{N}_{{\mathbb{Z}}_{4}^{-}}=s\mathcal{N}_{{\mathbb{Z}}_{4}^{+}}
define the following twisted partition functions at general
R\geq\sqrt{2}
,

\displaystyle Z_{1\mathcal{N}_{{\mathbb{Z}}_{4}^{+}}}(\tau,\bar{\tau})=

\displaystyle{\displaystyle 1\over|\eta|^{2}}\left(\sum_{m,n\in{\mathbb{Z}},m-n\in 2{\mathbb{Z}}}i^{n-m}q^{{1\over 2}\left({n\over R}+{mR\over 2}\right)^{2}}\bar{q}^{{1\over 2}\left({n\over R}-{mR\over 2}\right)^{2}}+\sum_{m,n\in{\mathbb{Z}}}(-1)^{m+n}q^{m^{2}}\bar{q}^{n^{2}}\right)\,,

(4.22)

\displaystyle Z_{1\mathcal{N}_{{\mathbb{Z}}_{4}^{-}}}(\tau,\bar{\tau})=

\displaystyle{\displaystyle 1\over|\eta|^{2}}\left(\sum_{m,n\in{\mathbb{Z}},m-n\in 2{\mathbb{Z}}}i^{n+m}q^{{1\over 2}\left({n\over R}+{mR\over 2}\right)^{2}}\bar{q}^{{1\over 2}\left({n\over R}-{mR\over 2}\right)^{2}}+\sum_{m,n\in{\mathbb{Z}}}(-1)^{m+n}q^{m^{2}}\bar{q}^{n^{2}}\right)\,.

Their modular
S
-transform (using Poisson resummation) yields the spectrum in the duality-twisted sectors,

\displaystyle Z_{\mathcal{N}_{{\mathbb{Z}}_{4}^{+}}1}(\tau,\bar{\tau})=

\displaystyle{\displaystyle 1\over 2|\eta|^{2}}\left(\sum_{m,n\in{\mathbb{Z}},m-n\in 2{\mathbb{Z}}}q^{{1\over 8}\left({n-1/2\over R}+{(m+1/2)R\over 2}\right)^{2}}\bar{q}^{{1\over 8}\left({n-1/2\over R}-{(m+1/2)R\over 2}\right)^{2}}+\sum_{m,n\in{\mathbb{Z}}}q^{{1\over 4}(m+1/2)^{2}}\bar{q}^{{1\over 4}(n+1/2)^{2}}\right)\,,

(4.23)

\displaystyle Z_{\mathcal{N}_{{\mathbb{Z}}_{4}^{-}}1}(\tau,\bar{\tau})=

\displaystyle{\displaystyle 1\over 2|\eta|^{2}}\left(\sum_{m,n\in{\mathbb{Z}},m-n\in 2{\mathbb{Z}}}q^{{1\over 8}\left({n+1/2\over R}+{(m+1/2)R\over 2}\right)^{2}}\bar{q}^{{1\over 8}\left({n+1/2\over R}-{(m+1/2)R\over 2}\right)^{2}}+\sum_{m,n\in{\mathbb{Z}}}q^{{1\over 4}(m+1/2)^{2}}\bar{q}^{{1\over 4}(n+1/2)^{2}}\right)\,,

which decompose into Virasoro characters with positive integer degeneracies, as required, thanks to the shifts in the exponents of
q
and
\bar{q}
.

We can also study the spin selection rules by specializing (
3.40
) to
k=4
. If we define the two inequivalent bicharacters as

\displaystyle\chi_{\pm}(a,b)=(\pm i)^{ab}\,,

(4.24)

then

\displaystyle\chi_{+},\epsilon=1:

\displaystyle e^{4\pi is}=\{1,\pm e^{-{\pi i\over 4}}\}\,,

(4.25)

\displaystyle\chi_{+},\epsilon=-1:

\displaystyle e^{4\pi is}=\{-1,\pm e^{-{\pi i\over 4}}\}\,,

\displaystyle\chi_{-},\epsilon=1:

\displaystyle e^{4\pi is}=\{1,\pm e^{{\pi i\over 4}}\}\,,

\displaystyle\chi_{-},\epsilon=-1:

\displaystyle e^{4\pi is}=\{-1,\pm e^{{\pi i\over 4}}\}\,.

Since the set of allowed spins
s
for the four
{\mathbb{Z}}_{4}
TY categories are non-identical, it provides a quick way to diagnose which
{\mathbb{Z}}_{4}
duality defect
\mathcal{N}
corresponds to by looking at the spins in
\mathcal{H}_{\mathcal{N}}
(equivalently the partition function
Z_{\mathcal{N}1}
). This allows us to determine the Frobenius-Schur indicator to be
\epsilon=1
for both
\mathbb{Z}_{4}
duality lines.

In Section
4.4
, we will return to these duality defects at the
\mathbb{Z}_{4}
parafermion point, where they come from chiral parity symmetries in the parafermion theory (see Section
2.6
), and explicitly verify the action on local operators given above. At the KT point, they become the
\mathbb{Z}_{k}
self-duality studied in Section
3.3
for
k=4
.

4.3
D_{4}
Tambara-Yamagami Symmetry

Let us denote the orbifold partition function at radius
R
coupled to
\mathbb{Z}_{2}\times\mathbb{Z}_{2}=D_{4}
gauge fields
A_{g}
,
A_{h}
on a closed spacetime
\Sigma
for
g,h\in D_{8}
commuting elements of order 2 as

Z_{\rm orb}(\Sigma,R,A_{g},A_{h})\,.

(4.26)

There are two choices of
g
and
h
up to conjugacy in
D_{8}
and automorphism of
D_{4}
, namely
g,h=s,sr^{2}
or
g,h=sr,sr^{3}
. The corresponding groups are denoted by
D_{4}^{A}
and the
D_{4}^{B}
respectively in (
4.14
).

This partition function is not uniquely defined by the action of
D_{4}
on local operators since there is a possible discrete torsion class in
H^{2}(D_{4},U(1))=\mathbb{Z}_{2}
which can modify the action of
g
on the
h
-twisted sector by a sign, and vice versa, meaning that the two partition functions on the torus
\Sigma=T^{2}
in the sector
A_{g}=(1,g)
(
g
-twist in time),
A_{h}=(h,1)
(
h
-twist in space) differ by an overall sign, corresponding to tensoring the theory with an SPT.

To fix the ambiguity, we look at the
g
-charge of the lightest operator in the
h
-twisted sector and choose the overall sign of
Z_{\rm orb}
by requiring this operator to be neutral, so that in the low-temperature expansion, restricting to
q=\bar{q}
, the leading term is positive,

Z_{\rm orb}(T^{2},R,(1,g),(h,1))=q^{\alpha}+\text{subleading terms}\qquad R>\sqrt{2}\,.

(4.27)

This makes sense for the
\mathbb{Z}_{2}
orbifold because as long as
R
is greater than its value at the KT point
R=\sqrt{2}
, there is a unique lightest operator in the
h
-twisted sector for any
\mathbb{Z}_{2}
symmetry generator
h
. For instance, with
h=r^{2}
, this operator is
e^{i\theta}-e^{-i\theta}
, of weight
h=\bar{h}={1\over 2R^{2}}
. At
R=\sqrt{2}
the above partition function vanishes because of the chiral anomaly.

It makes sense to define
Z_{\rm orb}(T^{2},R,A_{g},A_{h})
also for
R<\sqrt{2}
, since T-duality does not respect the
D_{4}
symmetries of the orbifold, as it acts by an outer automorphism of
D_{8}
taking
s\leftrightarrow sr
. As we continuously tune
R
through the KT point
R=\sqrt{2}
we find there is a level crossing in the
h
-twisted sector where two operators of opposite
g
-charge exchange places as the lightest operator in the
h
-twisted sector (this level crossing happens across the whole spectrum, giving the vanishing of the partition function we mentioned above). Thus, we have (again with
q=\bar{q}\ll 1
)

Z_{\rm orb}(T^{2},R,(1,g),(h,1))=-q^{\alpha}+\text{subleading terms}\qquad R<\sqrt{2}\,.

(4.28)

This type of phenomenon was dubbed a “topological transition” in
[
44
]
.

This level crossing is related to an anomalous accidental symmetry at the KT point related to T-duality which exchanges
D_{4}^{A}
and
D_{4}^{B}
(see Appendix
A.3
). This complicates the usual discussion of the duality. The correct statement is that if we take our theory so-defined enriched with its
D_{4}^{B}
symmetry and tune the marginal parameter
R
through the KT point to the dual value
2\over R
, then we find a theory which may be equivalently described as a
D_{4}^{A\star}
-enriched orbifold at radius
R
, where the star indicates discrete torsion.

We find that with the definitions above, the theory is self-dual under gauging
D_{4}^{B}
for all
R
,

Z_{\rm orb}(\Sigma,R,A_{sr},A_{sr^{3}})=\frac{1}{4}\sum_{B_{sr},B_{sr^{3}}\in H^{1}(\Sigma,\mathbb{Z}_{2})}(-1)^{\int_{\Sigma}A_{sr}B_{sr}+A_{sr^{3}}B_{sr^{3}}}Z_{\rm orb}(\Sigma,R,B_{sr},B_{sr^{3}})\,.

(4.29)

On the other hand, if we define

Z_{\rm orb}^{\star}(\Sigma,R,A_{g},A_{h})=(-1)^{\int_{\Sigma}A_{g}\cup A_{h}}Z_{\rm orb}(\Sigma,R,A_{g},A_{h})

(4.30)

to be the theory obtained by taking the opposite convention as what we have considered above, equivalent to tensoring
Z_{\rm orb}
with the
\mathbb{Z}_{2}\times\mathbb{Z}_{2}
SPT, we find instead it is self-dual under gauging
D_{4}^{A}
,

Z_{\rm orb}^{\star}(\Sigma,R,A_{s},A_{sr^{2}})=\frac{1}{4}\sum_{B_{s},B_{sr^{2}}\in H^{1}(\Sigma,\mathbb{Z}_{2})}(-1)^{\int_{\Sigma}A_{s}B_{s}+A_{sr^{2}}B_{sr^{2}}}Z_{\rm orb}^{\star}(\Sigma,R,B_{s},B_{sr^{2}})\,.

(4.31)

These two equations are related by T-duality and everything is consistent.

Therefore we expect these self-dualities to give rise to T-dual pairs of
D_{4}^{A}
and
D_{4}^{B}
TY symmetries.
22
22

22

The two
D_{4}
TY symmetries differ in their F-gauge. See discussions in Section
2.4
and around (
2.16
).
There are four such TY categories labeled by the FS indicator
\epsilon=\pm
of the duality defect and two bicharacters,
\chi_{s}
coming from the product of the unique bicharacters of each
\mathbb{Z}_{2}
factor and
\chi_{a}
related to
\chi_{s}
by swapping the two
\mathbb{Z}_{2}
factors,

\displaystyle\chi_{s}(i_{1},i_{2};j_{1},j_{2})=(-1)^{i_{1}j_{1}+i_{2}j_{2}}\,,\quad\chi_{a}(i_{1},i_{2};j_{1},j_{2})=(-1)^{i_{1}j_{2}+i_{2}j_{1}}\,,

(4.32)

where
i_{1,2},j_{1,2}\in{\mathbb{Z}}_{2}
.

The self-dualities above evidently correspond to the bicharacter
\chi_{s}
. We will see below that the FS indicator is
+
, so this gives rise to the TY category
{\rm TY}({\mathbb{Z}}_{2}\times{\mathbb{Z}}_{2},\chi_{s},+)={\rm Rep}(H_{8})
. We denote this duality line
\mathcal{N}_{H_{8},B}
and its T-dual
\mathcal{N}_{H_{8},A}
. Let us focus on
\mathcal{N}_{H_{8}}=\mathcal{N}_{H_{8},B}
. The element
s\in D_{8}
acts as the swapping automorphism of
D_{4}^{B}
, so fusing
\mathcal{N}_{H_{8}}
with the line corresponding to
s
yields
{\rm TY}({\mathbb{Z}}_{2}\times{\mathbb{Z}}_{2},\chi_{a},+)={\rm Rep}(D_{8})
,

\displaystyle{\rm TY}(D_{4}^{B},\chi_{s},+){\mathrel{\to}^{~~\mathcal{N}_{H_{8}}\ \mapsto\ s\mathcal{N}_{H_{8}}=\mathcal{N}_{D_{8}}~~}}{\rm TY}(D_{4}^{B},\chi_{a},+)\,.

(4.33)

We propose the following action on local operators

\displaystyle\widehat{\mathcal{N}}_{D_{8}}:\begin{cases}V^{+}_{n,w}\to 2i^{n}V_{n,w}^{+}\quad{~\rm for~}n\in 2{\mathbb{Z}}\,,\\
j_{n^{2}}\bar{j}_{m^{2}}\to 2j_{n^{2}}\bar{j}_{m^{2}}\,,\\
\text{other primaries}\to\text{non-local operators}\,,\end{cases}

(4.34)

\displaystyle\widehat{\mathcal{N}}_{H_{8}}:\begin{cases}V^{+}_{n,w}\to 2i^{n}(-1)^{w}V_{n,w}^{+}\quad{~\rm for~}n\in 2{\mathbb{Z}}\,,\\
j_{n^{2}}\bar{j}_{m^{2}}\to 2j_{n^{2}}\bar{j}_{m^{2}}\,,\\
\text{other primaries}\to\text{non-local operators}\,.\end{cases}

(4.35)

These satisfy the desired TY fusion rules. The T-dual lines for the
D_{4}^{A}
self-duality act as above by switching
n
and
w
.

The twisted torus partition functions associated with this action are

\displaystyle Z_{1\mathcal{N}_{D_{8}}}(\tau,\bar{\tau})=

\displaystyle{\displaystyle 1\over|\eta|^{2}}\left(\sum_{m,n\in{\mathbb{Z}}}(-1)^{n}q^{{1\over 2}\left({2n\over R}+{mR\over 2}\right)^{2}}\bar{q}^{{1\over 2}\left({2n\over R}-{mR\over 2}\right)^{2}}+\sum_{m,n\in{\mathbb{Z}}}(-1)^{m+n}q^{m^{2}}\bar{q}^{n^{2}}\right)\,,

(4.36)

\displaystyle Z_{1\mathcal{N}_{H_{8}}}(\tau,\bar{\tau})=

\displaystyle{\displaystyle 1\over|\eta|^{2}}\left(\sum_{m,n\in{\mathbb{Z}}}(-1)^{m+n}q^{{1\over 2}\left({2n\over R}+{mR\over 2}\right)^{2}}\bar{q}^{{1\over 2}\left({2n\over R}-{mR\over 2}\right)^{2}}+\sum_{m,n\in{\mathbb{Z}}}(-1)^{m+n}q^{m^{2}}\bar{q}^{n^{2}}\right)\,.

These solve the modular bootstrap equation (
2.5
) for the torus partition functions with one duality line inserted.
In particular, the modular
S
-transform (from Poisson resummation formula) yields the duality-twisted sector spectra

\displaystyle Z_{\mathcal{N}_{D_{8}}1}(\tau,\bar{\tau})=

\displaystyle{\displaystyle 1\over 2|\eta|^{2}}\left(\sum_{m,n\in{\mathbb{Z}}}q^{{1\over 2}\left({n\over R}+{(m+1/2)R\over 4}\right)^{2}}\bar{q}^{{1\over 2}\left({n\over R}-{(m+1/2)R\over 4}\right)^{2}}+\sum_{m,n\in{\mathbb{Z}}}q^{{1\over 4}(m+1/2)^{2}}\bar{q}^{{1\over 4}(n+1/2)^{2}}\right)\,,

(4.37)

\displaystyle Z_{\mathcal{N}_{H_{8}}1}(\tau,\bar{\tau})=

\displaystyle{\displaystyle 1\over 2|\eta|^{2}}\left(\sum_{m,n\in{\mathbb{Z}}}q^{{1\over 2}\left({n+1/2\over R}+{(m+1/2)R\over 4}\right)^{2}}\bar{q}^{{1\over 2}\left({n+1/2\over R}-{(m+1/2)R\over 4}\right)^{2}}+\sum_{m,n\in{\mathbb{Z}}}q^{{1\over 4}(m+1/2)^{2}}\bar{q}^{{1\over 4}(n+1/2)^{2}}\right),

which thanks to the shifts in the exponents of
q
and
\bar{q}
decomposes into Virasoro characters with positive integer degeneracies, as required.

The TY data also shows up in the spin selection rules (similar to those derived in Section
3.3.3
). For a general
D_{4}
TY defect
\mathcal{N}
satisfying
\mathcal{N}^{2}=1+\eta+\sigma+\eta\sigma
we have

\displaystyle\chi_{s}:~e^{4\pi is}\langle\psi^{\prime}|\psi\rangle=

\displaystyle{\epsilon\over 2}\langle\psi^{\prime}|(1+\widehat{\eta}_{-}+\widehat{\sigma}_{-}+\widehat{\eta}_{-}\widehat{\sigma}_{-}|\psi\rangle\,,

(4.38)

\displaystyle\chi_{a}:~e^{4\pi is}\langle\psi^{\prime}|\psi\rangle=

\displaystyle{\epsilon\over 2}\langle\psi^{\prime}|(1+\widehat{\eta}_{-}+\widehat{\sigma}_{-}-\widehat{\eta}_{-}\widehat{\sigma}_{-}|\psi\rangle\,,

where we have used that
\eta_{-},\sigma_{-}
commute (regardless of the choice of bicharacter). Moreover from crossing relations we have

\displaystyle\chi_{s}:~\widehat{\eta}_{-}^{2}=\widehat{\sigma}_{-}^{2}=-1\,,

(4.39)

\displaystyle\chi_{a}:~\widehat{\eta}_{-}^{2}=\widehat{\sigma}_{-}^{2}=1\,.

Consequently, the spin selection rules are

\displaystyle\chi_{s},\epsilon=1:

\displaystyle e^{4\pi is}=\{1,i,-i\}\,,

(4.40)

\displaystyle\chi_{s},\epsilon=-1:

\displaystyle e^{4\pi is}=\{-1,i,-i\}\,,

\displaystyle\chi_{a},\epsilon=1:

\displaystyle e^{4\pi is}=\{1,-1\}\,,

\displaystyle\chi_{a},\epsilon=-1:

\displaystyle e^{4\pi is}=\{1,-1\}\,.

This provides a quick way to determine whether the crossing data is associated to
\chi_{s}
or
\chi_{a}
. The spin selection rules differentiate the two categories
{\rm TY}(D_{4},\chi_{s},\pm)
but a more refined argument is needed to determine the FS indicator in
{\rm TY}(D_{4},\chi_{a},\pm)
.

We will verify the action of local operators in Section
4.5
by studying Verlinde lines at the Ising
2
point and in Section
4.6
at the four-state Potts point. This will also determine the Frobenius-Schur indicators.

4.4
{\mathbb{Z}}_{4}
Tambara-Yamagami and
{\mathbb{Z}}_{4}
Parafermions

Let us consider the
\mathbb{Z}_{2}
orbifold at the special radius
R=\sqrt{6}
. This theory is rational and its RCFT structure is manifest from its
SU(2)_{4}/U(1)
coset description of the
A_{5}
type (diagonal modular invariant),
23
23

23

In the following all
SU(2)_{k}/U(1)
coset CFTs are defined with respect to the diagonal modular invariant unless otherwise noted.

\displaystyle{U(1)_{6}\over{\mathbb{Z}}_{2}^{C}}\cong{SU(2)_{4}^{A_{5}}\over U(1)}\,.

(4.41)

The same CFT is also realized by bosonization from the
{\mathbb{Z}}_{4}
parafermions
[
56
]
(see also Appendix
B
).
We will see that the
\mathbb{Z}_{4}
TY TDLs outlined in Section
4.2
consist of Verlinde lines (see Section
2.2
) at this point together with the duality TDLs coming from chiral symmetries for the
{\mathbb{Z}}_{4}
parafermions (see Section
2.6
).
This also provides a check on the proposed actions (
4.21
) of dualities on local operators on the orbifold branch.

The relevant chiral algebra is given by the W-algebra
W(2,3,4)
which includes spin 3 and 4 generators,

\displaystyle\cos({\sqrt{6}X_{L}})=\cos(3\theta+\phi)\quad{\rm and}\quad j_{4}\,,

(4.42)

in addition to the stress tensor.
With respect to
W(2,3,4)
, the
SU(2)_{k}/U(1)
coset CFT has 10 primaries
\phi^{j}_{m}
as listed in Table
7
with
j=0,{1\over 2},1,{3\over 2},2
and
m+j\in{\mathbb{Z}}
and subject to the identification

\displaystyle\phi^{j}_{m}=\phi^{j}_{m+4}=\phi^{2-j}_{2+m}\,.

(4.43)

Correspondingly there are 10 TDLs in the
SU(2)_{4}/U(1)
CFT realized by Verlinde lines
\mathcal{L}_{(j,m)}
. In particular, from the fusion rules, we find that the
{\mathbb{Z}}_{4}
subgroup of
D_{8}
is realized by the Verlinde lines as
r^{i}=\mathcal{L}_{(0,i)}
for
i=0,1,2,3
.
