Table 8:
Global symmetries of the
{\mathbb{Z}}_{k}
parafermion theory.

The full global symmetry of the parafermion theory is,

\displaystyle G_{PF}=({\mathbb{Z}}_{k}\times\bar{\mathbb{Z}}_{k})\rtimes({\mathbb{Z}}_{2}^{C}\times{\mathbb{Z}}_{2}^{S})\,,

(B.24)

where
{\mathbb{Z}}_{2}^{C}
and
{\mathbb{Z}}_{2}^{S}
act on the
{\mathbb{Z}}_{k}\times\bar{\mathbb{Z}}_{k}
by conjugating and swapping the two factors respectively. The operators of the parafermion theory transform under (
B.24
) as summarized in Table
8
. Here
\bar{\mathbb{Z}}_{k}
is the generalization of the
(-1)^{F}
symmetry at
k=2
, while
{\mathbb{Z}}_{k}
is identified with the
{\mathbb{Z}}_{k}^{B}
symmetry of the bosonized theory.
38
38

38

For notational simplicity,
{\mathbb{Z}}_{k}^{B}
and
{\mathbb{Z}}_{2}^{C}
are denoted as
{\mathbb{Z}}_{k}
and
{\mathbb{Z}}_{2}
in the last section.
The bosonization of the parafermion theory amounts to summing over
\bar{\mathbb{Z}}_{k}
twisted sectors and projecting to the
\bar{\mathbb{Z}}_{k}
invariant states. Consequently, the bosonized theory only contain operators of the form
\phi^{j}_{m,m}
, which constituents the diagonal modular invariant (of the
A_{k+1}
type) for the
SU(2)_{k}/U(1)
coset CFT. The other more general parafermion operators carry nonzero
\bar{\mathbb{Z}}_{k}
charges reside in the
{\mathbb{Z}}_{k}^{B}
twisted sectors, as reviewed in the last section.

The full global symmetry of the bosonic
SU(2)_{k}/U(1)
coset CFT is given in (
B.6
) and we see the
{\mathbb{Z}}_{2}
conjugation is also inherited from the
{\mathbb{Z}}_{2}^{C}
symmetry in the parafermion theory. The
{\mathbb{Z}}_{2}^{S}
symmetry in (
B.24
) however does not lead to an ordinary symmetry in the bosonized theory. For
k=2
, this is simply the left fermion parity
(-1)^{F_{L}}
which is known to generate the Kramers-Wannier duality under bosonization. Here we will see that for general
k
, the
{\mathbb{Z}}_{2}^{S}
symmetry of the
{\mathbb{Z}}_{k}
parafermions corresponds to a generalized Kramers-Wannier duality for
SU(2)_{k}/U(1)
coset CFTs, namely self-duality under gauging the
{\mathbb{Z}}_{k}^{B}
symmetry with the bicharacter
\chi_{+}(a,b)={2\pi ab\over k}
, as evident from the first equality in (
B.17
) satisfied by the torus partition function in general
{\mathbb{Z}}_{k}^{B}
background.

The parafermionic partition function with the paraspin structure
(s_{1},s_{2})
on
T^{2}
is obtained from (
2.31
) applied to (
B.16
)
[
54
]
,

\displaystyle Z_{PF}(\tau,\bar{\tau};s_{1},s_{2})={1\over 2}|\eta(\tau)|^{2}\sum_{a=0}^{k-1}\sum_{\ell=0}^{k}\omega^{-(a+s_{1})s_{2}}\left(c^{\ell}_{s_{1}}(\tau)c^{\ell}_{s_{1}+2a}(\bar{\tau})+c^{\ell}_{s_{1}+k}(\tau)c^{\ell}_{s_{1}+k+2a}(\bar{\tau})\right)\,,

(B.25)

which counts the states in the twisted sector
\mathcal{H}_{s_{1}}^{PF}
with an insertion of the
{\mathbb{Z}}_{k}
group element corresponding to
s_{2}
along the spatial cycle of the
T^{2}
.
The condition (
2.35
) for self-duality in the parafermion theory, after projecting to sectors with definite
\bar{\mathbb{Z}}_{k}
charges, simply requires a symmetry between states of charge
q
and
s-q
in the twisted sector
\mathcal{H}_{s}^{PF}
, which follows from the
{\mathbb{Z}}_{2}^{S}
symmetry in Table
8
. Furthermore, the
{\mathbb{Z}}_{2}^{S}
symmetry exchanges the order spin operators
\sigma_{n}
and disorder spin operators
\mu_{n}
, which is expected for the
{\mathbb{Z}}_{k}
duality since the latter live in the
{\mathbb{Z}}_{k}^{B}
twisted sector of the bosonic coset CFT. Finally the
{\mathbb{Z}}_{k}^{B}
symmetric operators (i.e. the energy operators
\epsilon_{r}
) also carry definite parity under the duality
[
92
]
.
The duality defect is not a Verlinde line in the
SU(2)_{k}/U(1)
coset CFT since
{\mathbb{Z}}_{2}^{S}
acts nontrivially on the chiral algebra
{\mathcal{V}}_{k}
(
B.7
). Under the duality, all left-moving generators are invariant, whereas on the right-moving side, the generators of spin
\ell
transform by a sign
(-1)^{\ell}
. This needs to be taken into account when writing down the duality twisted partition functions of the coset CFT. A similar analysis as above applies to the diagonal
{\mathbb{Z}}_{2}\subset{\mathbb{Z}}_{2}^{C}\times{\mathbb{Z}}_{2}^{S}
symmetry which leads to the condition (
2.37
), corresponds to the second self-duality in the coset CFT, and explains the second equality in (
B.17
). For this latter self-duality, it is the left-moving chiral algebra generators that transform with a sign
(-1)^{\ell}
.

B.4
The duality defects and
{\mathbb{Z}}_{k}
Tambara-Yamagami Symmetries

As explained in the last section, the
{\mathbb{Z}}_{2}^{S}
symmetry generator
S
of the
{\mathbb{Z}}_{k}
parafermions, upon bosonization, gives rise to a
{\mathbb{Z}}_{k}
duality defect
\mathcal{N}
. For
k>2
the same procedure also works for the other chiral symmetry
CS
where
C
generates the
{\mathbb{Z}}_{2}^{C}
in (
B.24
). Therefore we have in fact two such duality defects
\mathcal{N}
and
C\mathcal{N}
in the
SU(2)_{k}/U(1)
coset CFT, which lead to the two equalities between the
{\mathbb{Z}}_{k}
twisted partition functions in (
B.17
) following the general discussion in Section
2.4
.
Since
C
acts as an automorphism on
{\mathbb{Z}}_{k}
, the two duality defects have identical fusion rules and generate two
{\mathbb{Z}}_{k}
TY symmetries.

Such fusion categories are classified in
[
36
]
.
For
k>2
, they are parametrized by the bicharacter
e^{i\chi(a,b)}=\omega^{ab}
where
\omega
is a primitive
k
-root of unity and the Frobenius-Schur indicator
\epsilon=\pm
. Comparing the
{\mathbb{Z}}_{k}
and
\bar{\mathbb{Z}}_{k}
charges of the order and disorder spin operators given in Table
8
that are related by the parafermionic symmetries
S
and
CS
, we see that the corresponding bicharacters for the duality defects
\mathcal{N}
and
C\mathcal{N}
are
\chi_{+}
and
\chi_{-}
respectively (see Section
2.4
).
Below we provide further evidence that the TY symmetries in the coset CFT correspond to the two categories
{\rm TY}({\mathbb{Z}}_{k},\chi_{\pm},1)
(see Table
4
).

Firstly, the two duality TDLs
\mathcal{N}
and
C\mathcal{N}
must have the identical FS indicator but conjugate bicharacters. The former follows since
C
has trivial F-symbols (no ’t Hooft anomaly) whereas the latter can be derived from F-moves as in
[
14
]
(see Figure
13
).
39
39

39

We note that these relations between the F-symbols of the underlying fusion categories do not depend on specifics of the underlying CFT and it would be interesting to understand more general relations of this type.
Next, the spin spectrum in the defect Hilbert space
\mathcal{H}_{\mathcal{N}}
(or
\mathcal{H}_{C\mathcal{N}}
) of the duality TDL is constrained by both
\chi
and
\epsilon
. For
\chi=\chi_{\pm}
, the spin selection rules are given in (
3.45
),

\displaystyle e^{4\pi is}=\begin{cases}\epsilon e^{\pm{\pi i\over 4k}(2n+1)^{2}}e^{\mp\pi i/4}&k\in 2{\mathbb{Z}}\,,\\
\epsilon e^{\pm{\pi i\over k}n^{2}}e^{\mp\pi i/4}&k\in 2{\mathbb{Z}}+1\,.\end{cases}

(B.26)

Therefore knowledge of the partition function for the defect Hilbert space
\mathcal{H}_{\mathcal{N}}
can be used to fix
\epsilon
. For
k=2,3
, by inspecting the spin spectrum of
\mathcal{H}_{\mathcal{N}}
in the Ising and 3-state Potts CFT, one finds that
\epsilon=1

[
12
]
. For
k=4
, we deduce that
\epsilon=1
from the bootstrap analysis in Section
3.3.4
. We conjecture that the duality defects for general
k
in the
SU(2)_{k}/U(1)
coset CFT have
\epsilon=1
. This can be proven for example by working out the duality twisted partition functions
Z_{1\mathcal{N}}
and we leave that to the interested readers.

e^{i\chi_{\mathcal{N}}(g_{1},g_{2}^{-1})}
e^{i\chi_{C\mathcal{N}}(g_{1},g_{2})}

Figure 13:
Two sequences of F-moves that go from the TDL configuration in the leftmost figure to the top figure. The equivalence between them relates the bicharacters associated to the two duality defects
\mathcal{N}
and
C\mathcal{N}
by
e^{i\chi_{C\mathcal{N}}(g_{1},g_{2})}=e^{i\chi_{\mathcal{N}}(g_{1},g_{2}^{-1})}=e^{-i\chi_{\mathcal{N}}(g_{1},g_{2})}
.

Appendix C
c=1
CFT along the orbifold branch and
D_{8}
discrete gauging

At a generic point along the orbifold branch of the
c=1
moduli space, the CFT has
D_{8}
global symmetry. Here we consider discrete gauging by subgroups of
D_{8}
.

We adopt the standard representation
D_{8}=\langle r,s|r^{4}=s^{2}=(sr)^{2}=1\rangle
, whose conjugacy classes and corresponding stabilizers are

\displaystyle\{1\}:

\displaystyle D_{8}\,,

(C.1)

\displaystyle\{r^{2}\}:

\displaystyle D_{8}\,,

\displaystyle\{s,r^{2}s\}:

\displaystyle{\displaystyle\mathbb{Z}}_{2}\times{\mathbb{Z}}_{2}=\{1,r^{2},s,r^{2}s\}\,,

\displaystyle\{rs,r^{3}s\}:

\displaystyle{\displaystyle\mathbb{Z}}_{2}\times{\mathbb{Z}}_{2}=\{1,r^{2},rs,r^{3}s\}\,,

\displaystyle\{r,r^{3}\}:

\displaystyle{\displaystyle\mathbb{Z}}_{4}=\{1,r,r^{2},r^{3}\}\,.

The distinct gaugings of the CFT will be labeled by subgroups
G\subset D_{8}
up to conjugation, and possible discrete torsion classes from
H^{2}(G,U(1))
which we will refer to as
\iota(G)\in{\mathbb{Z}}_{2}
. In particular, we have 3 conjugacy classes of
{\mathbb{Z}}_{2}
subgroups, 2 conjugacy classes of
{\mathbb{Z}}_{2}\times{\mathbb{Z}}_{2}
subgroups (which we will label as
A
and
B
types) and 1 conjugacy class of
{\mathbb{Z}}_{4}
subgroup. As we will see, depending on these data, the discrete gauging may map one point on the orbifold branch with partition function
Z_{\rm orb}(R_{1})
to a point on the unorbifolded branch
Z_{\rm circ}(R_{2})
or another point on the orbifold branch
Z_{\rm orb}(R_{2})
. Let us briefly summarize our findings below and the details will be giving in the subsequent sections.

\displaystyle Z_{\rm orb}(R)\xrightarrow{{\mathbb{Z}}_{2}^{r^{2}}}Z_{\rm circ}(R)\,,

(C.2)

\displaystyle Z_{\rm orb}(R)\xrightarrow{{\mathbb{Z}}_{2}^{s}}Z_{\rm orb}(2R)\,,\quad Z_{\rm orb}(R)\xrightarrow{{\mathbb{Z}}_{2}^{sr}}Z_{\rm orb}(R/2)\,,

\displaystyle Z_{\rm orb}(R)\xrightarrow[\iota=0]{({\mathbb{Z}}_{2}\times{\mathbb{Z}}_{2})_{A}}Z_{\rm circ}(2R)\,,\quad Z_{\rm orb}(R)\xrightarrow[\iota=0]{({\mathbb{Z}}_{2}\times{\mathbb{Z}}_{2})_{B}}Z_{\rm orb}(R)\,,

\displaystyle Z_{\rm orb}(R)\xrightarrow[\iota=1]{({\mathbb{Z}}_{2}\times{\mathbb{Z}}_{2})_{A}}Z_{\rm orb}(R)\,,\quad Z_{\rm orb}(R)\xrightarrow[\iota=1]{({\mathbb{Z}}_{2}\times{\mathbb{Z}}_{2})_{B}}Z_{\rm circ}(2R)\,,

\displaystyle Z_{\rm orb}(R)\xrightarrow{{\mathbb{Z}}_{4}}Z_{\rm orb}(R)\,,\quad

\displaystyle Z_{\rm orb}(R)\xrightarrow[\iota=0]{D_{8}}Z_{\rm orb}(2R)\,,\quad Z_{\rm orb}(R)\xrightarrow[\iota=1]{D_{8}}Z_{\rm orb}(R/2)\,.

Note that T-duality acts on this set of transformation rules by swapping
A
and
B
,
s
and
sr
,
\iota=0
and
\iota=1
.

C.1
The
D_{8}
twisted torus partition functions

We label the torus partition function in the
r^{a}
twisted sector with a
sr^{b}
symmetry line inserted along the spatial cycle as
Z_{a\bar{b}}
. Similarly we define the twisted partition functions

Z_{ab},Z_{\bar{a}\bar{b}},Z_{\bar{a}b}
.

Let
{\mathcal{Z}}^{{\alpha}{\beta}}_{{\gamma}{\delta}}
denote twisted partition function of the (unorbifolded)
c=1
compact boson at radius
R
:

\displaystyle{\mathcal{Z}}^{{\alpha}{\beta}}_{{\gamma}{\delta}}(\tau,R,\omega)={1\over|\eta|^{2}}\sum_{n,m\in{\mathbb{Z}}}\omega^{(2n+{\gamma})(2m+{\delta})}q^{{1\over 2}\left({n+{\alpha}/2\over R}+{(m+{\beta}/2)R\over 2}\right)^{2}}\bar{q}^{{1\over 2}\left({n+{\alpha}/2\over R}-{(m+{\beta}/2)R\over 2}\right)^{2}}\,,

(C.3)

with
\omega=e^{\pi i\over 2}
. Under
T
and
S
transformations, they transform as

\displaystyle{\mathcal{Z}}^{{\alpha}{\beta}}_{{\gamma}{\delta}}(\tau+1,R,\omega)=

\displaystyle\omega^{-{\alpha}{\delta}-{\beta}{\gamma}}{\mathcal{Z}}^{{\alpha}+{\gamma},{\beta}+{\delta}}_{{\gamma}{\delta}}(\tau,R,\omega)\,,

(C.4)

\displaystyle{\mathcal{Z}}^{{\alpha}{\beta}}_{{\gamma}{\delta}}(-1/\tau,R,\omega)=

\displaystyle\omega^{({\alpha}-{\gamma})({\beta}-{\delta})+2{\alpha}{\beta}}{\mathcal{Z}}^{{\gamma}{\delta}}_{{\alpha}{\beta}}(\tau,R,\omega)\,.

We also recall
[
75
]

\displaystyle W\equiv{1\over\eta}\sum_{r\in{\mathbb{Z}}}(-1)^{r}q^{r^{2}}\,,\quad W_{\pm}\equiv{1\over\eta}\sum_{r\in{\mathbb{Z}}}(\pm 1)^{r}q^{\left(r+{1\over 4}\right)^{2}}\,.

(C.5)

which transform as

\displaystyle T:\begin{pmatrix}\sqrt{1\over 2}W(\tau+1)\\
W_{+}(\tau+1)\\
W_{-}(\tau+1)\end{pmatrix}=\begin{pmatrix}e^{-2\pi i/24}&0&0\\
0&e^{\pi i/24}&0\\
0&0&e^{\pi i/24}\end{pmatrix}\begin{pmatrix}\sqrt{1\over 2}W(\tau)\\
W_{-}(\tau)\\
W_{+}(\tau)\end{pmatrix}\,,

(C.6)

\displaystyle S:\begin{pmatrix}\sqrt{1\over 2}W(-1/\tau)\\
W_{+}(-1/\tau)\\
W_{-}(-1/\tau)\end{pmatrix}=\begin{pmatrix}0&1&0\\
1&0&0\\
0&0&1\end{pmatrix}\begin{pmatrix}\sqrt{1\over 2}W(\tau)\\
W_{+}(\tau)\\
W_{-}(\tau)\end{pmatrix}\,.

The explicit forms of these twisted torus partition functions of the
\mathbb{Z}_{2}
orbifold are

\displaystyle Z_{0\bar{0}}=

\displaystyle{\displaystyle 1\over 2}{\mathcal{Z}}_{00}^{10}+{1\over 2}|W|^{2}\,,~Z_{\bar{0}0}={1\over 2}{\mathcal{Z}}_{10}^{00}+|W_{+}|^{2}\,,~Z_{\bar{0}\bar{0}}={1\over 2}{\mathcal{Z}}_{10}^{10}+|W_{-}|^{2}\,,

(C.7)

\displaystyle Z_{2\bar{0}}=

\displaystyle{\displaystyle 1\over 2}{\mathcal{Z}}_{00}^{10}-{1\over 2}|W|^{2}\,,~Z_{\bar{0}2}={1\over 2}{\mathcal{Z}}_{10}^{00}-|W_{+}|^{2}\,,~Z_{\bar{0}\bar{2}}={1\over 2}{\mathcal{Z}}_{10}^{10}-|W_{-}|^{2}\,,

\displaystyle Z_{0\bar{1}}=

\displaystyle{\displaystyle 1\over 2}{\mathcal{Z}}_{00}^{01}+{1\over 2}|W|^{2}\,,~Z_{\bar{1}0}={1\over 2}{\mathcal{Z}}_{01}^{00}+|W_{+}|^{2}\,,~Z_{2\bar{1}}=-{1\over 2}{\mathcal{Z}}_{00}^{01}+{1\over 2}|W|^{2}\,,~Z_{\bar{1}2}=Z_{\bar{3}2}=-{1\over 2}{\mathcal{Z}}_{01}^{00}+|W_{+}|^{2}\,,

\displaystyle Z_{\bar{1}\bar{1}}=

\displaystyle{\displaystyle 1\over 2}{\mathcal{Z}}_{01}^{01}+|W_{-}|^{2}\,,~Z_{\bar{3}\bar{1}}=-{1\over 2}{\mathcal{Z}}_{01}^{01}+|W_{-}|^{2}\,,~

\displaystyle Z_{00}=

\displaystyle{\displaystyle 1\over 2}{\mathcal{Z}}_{00}^{00}+{1\over 2}|W|^{2}+|W_{+}|^{2}+|W_{-}|^{2}\,,~Z_{20}={1\over 2}{\mathcal{Z}}_{00}^{00}-{1\over 2}|W|^{2}+|W_{+}|^{2}-|W_{-}|^{2}\,,~

\displaystyle Z_{02}=

\displaystyle{\displaystyle 1\over 2}{\mathcal{Z}}_{00}^{00}+{1\over 2}|W|^{2}-|W_{+}|^{2}-|W_{-}|^{2}\,,~Z_{22}={1\over 2}{\mathcal{Z}}_{00}^{00}-{1\over 2}|W|^{2}-|W_{+}|^{2}+|W_{-}|^{2}\,,~

\displaystyle{Z_{10}}=

\displaystyle{\displaystyle Z_{30}={1\over 2}{\mathcal{Z}}_{11}^{00}+|W_{+}|^{2}}\,,~Z_{01}=Z_{03}={1\over 2}\omega^{-1}{\mathcal{Z}}_{00}^{11}+{1\over 2}|W|^{2}\,,~

\displaystyle Z_{12}=

\displaystyle-{1\over 2}{\mathcal{Z}}_{11}^{00}+|W_{+}|^{2}\,,~{Z_{21}}={1\over 2}\omega{\mathcal{Z}}_{00}^{11}+{1\over 2}|W|^{2}\,,~

\displaystyle Z_{13}=

\displaystyle-{1\over 2}{\mathcal{Z}}_{11}^{11}+|W_{-}|^{2}\,,~Z_{23}={1\over 2}\omega{\mathcal{Z}}_{00}^{11}+{1\over 2}|W|^{2}\,,~

\displaystyle{Z_{31}}=

\displaystyle{\displaystyle-{1\over 2}{\mathcal{Z}}_{11}^{11}+|W_{-}|^{2}}\,,~Z_{32}=-{1\over 2}{\mathcal{Z}}_{11}^{00}+|W_{+}|^{2}\,,~

\displaystyle{Z_{11}=}

\displaystyle{{\displaystyle 1\over 2}{\mathcal{Z}}_{11}^{11}+|W_{-}|^{2}}\,,~Z_{33}={1\over 2}{\mathcal{Z}}_{11}^{11}+|W_{-}|^{2}\,.

Note the following identities since
Z(g,h)=Z(cgc^{-1},chc^{-1})
,

\displaystyle Z_{10}=Z_{30}\,,~Z_{\bar{1}0}=Z_{\bar{3}0}\,,~Z_{\bar{0}0}=Z_{\bar{2}0}\,,~Z_{\bar{0}2}=Z_{\bar{2}2}\,,~Z_{\bar{\bar{1}}2}=Z_{\bar{3}2}\,,~

(C.8)

\displaystyle Z_{\bar{2}\bar{2}}=Z_{\bar{0}\bar{0}}\,,~Z_{\bar{0}\bar{2}}=Z_{\bar{2}\bar{0}}

and that under S-transform

\displaystyle Z_{11}(-1/\tau,-1/\bar{\tau})=Z_{31}(\tau,\bar{\tau})\,,

(C.9)

since the TDLs are oriented.

C.2
{\mathbb{Z}}_{2}
gauging

The
{\mathbb{Z}}_{2}
subgroups of
D_{8}
fall into 3 conjugate classes whose representatives are generated by
r^{2}
(center
{\mathbb{Z}}_{2}
),
s
or
rs
.

The center
{\mathbb{Z}}_{2}
orbifold returns the original partition function between
{\mathbb{Z}}_{2}
orbifold

\displaystyle Z^{r^{2}}_{{\mathbb{Z}}_{2}}(R)={1\over 2}(Z_{00}+Z_{02}+Z_{20}+Z_{22})=Z^{00}_{00}(R)=Z_{\rm circ}(R)\,.

(C.10)

The reflection
{\mathbb{Z}}_{2}
orbifold preserves the orbifold branch but increases or decreases
R
by 2 respectively

\displaystyle Z^{s}_{{\mathbb{Z}}_{2}}(R)=

\displaystyle{\displaystyle 1\over 2}(Z_{00}+Z_{0\bar{0}}+Z_{\bar{0}0}+Z_{\bar{0}\bar{0}})

(C.11)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle{\displaystyle 1\over 4}({\mathcal{Z}}_{00}^{00}+Z^{10}_{00}+Z^{00}_{10}+Z^{10}_{10})+{1\over 2}|W|^{2}+|W_{+}|^{2}+|W_{-}|^{2}=Z(2R)\,,

\displaystyle Z^{rs}_{{\mathbb{Z}}_{2}}(R)=

\displaystyle{\displaystyle 1\over 2}(Z_{00}+Z_{0\bar{1}}+Z_{\bar{1}0}+Z_{\bar{1}\bar{1}})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle{\displaystyle 1\over 4}({\mathcal{Z}}_{00}^{00}+Z^{01}_{00}+Z^{00}_{01}+Z^{01}_{01})+{1\over 2}|W|^{2}+|W_{+}|^{2}+|W_{-}|^{2}=Z(R/2)\,.

Here we have used

\displaystyle{\displaystyle\mathcal{Z}}_{00}^{00}+{\mathcal{Z}}^{01}_{00}+{\mathcal{Z}}^{00}_{01}+{\mathcal{Z}}^{01}_{01}

(C.12)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle{\displaystyle 1\over|\eta|^{2}}\sum_{n,m\in{\mathbb{Z}}}(1+(-)^{n})q^{{1\over 2}\left({n\over R}+{mR\over 2}\right)^{2}}\bar{q}^{{1\over 2}\left({n\over R}-{mR\over 2}\right)^{2}}+(1+(-)^{n})q^{{1\over 2}\left({n\over R}+{(m+1/2)R\over 2}\right)^{2}}\bar{q}^{{1\over 2}\left({n\over R}-{(m+1/2)R\over 2}\right)^{2}}\,,

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle{\displaystyle 2\over|\eta|^{2}}\sum_{n,m\in{\mathbb{Z}}}q^{{1\over 2}\left({2n\over R}+{mR\over 4}\right)^{2}}\bar{q}^{{1\over 2}\left({2n\over R}-{mR\over 4}\right)^{2}}\,.

C.3
{\mathbb{Z}}_{4}
gauging

The orbifold branch is point-wise invariant under
{\mathbb{Z}}_{4}
gauging

\displaystyle Z_{{\mathbb{Z}}_{4}}(R)={1\over 4}\sum_{a,b=0}^{3}Z_{ab}={1\over 4}(2Z^{00}_{00}+2|W|^{2}+4|W_{+}|^{2}+4|W_{+}|^{2})=Z_{00}(R)=Z(R)

(C.13)

C.4
{\mathbb{Z}}_{2}\times{\mathbb{Z}}_{2}
gauging

There are two
{\mathbb{Z}}_{2}\times{\mathbb{Z}}_{2}
subgroups of
D_{8}
up to conjugation. We will refer to them as

\displaystyle({\mathbb{Z}}_{2}\times{\mathbb{Z}}_{2})^{A}=

\displaystyle\{1,r^{2},s,r^{2}s\}\,,

(C.14)

\displaystyle({\mathbb{Z}}_{2}\times{\mathbb{Z}}_{2})^{B}=

\displaystyle\{1,r^{2},rs,r^{3}s\}\,.

Furthermore
H^{2}({\mathbb{Z}}_{2}\times{\mathbb{Z}}_{2},U(1))={\mathbb{Z}}_{2}
, so we may couple a nontrivial SPT when gauging.

Trivial SPT

In one case the orbifold branch is mapped to the unorbifolded branch with
R
doubled

\displaystyle Z^{A}_{{\mathbb{Z}}_{2}\times{\mathbb{Z}}_{2}}=

\displaystyle{\displaystyle 1\over 2}(Z_{0\bar{0}}+Z_{2\bar{0}}+Z_{\bar{0}0}+Z_{\bar{0}2}+Z_{\bar{0}\bar{0}}+Z_{\bar{0}\bar{2}})+{1\over 4}(Z_{00}+Z_{02}+Z_{20}+Z_{22})\,,

(C.15)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle{\displaystyle 1\over 2}({\mathcal{Z}}_{00}^{00}+{\mathcal{Z}}_{10}^{00}+{\mathcal{Z}}_{00}^{10}+{\mathcal{Z}}_{10}^{10})\,,

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle Z^{00}_{00}(2R)=Z_{\rm circ}(2R)\,,

while in the other case the orbifold branch is invariant

\displaystyle Z^{B}_{{\mathbb{Z}}_{2}\times{\mathbb{Z}}_{2}}(R)=

\displaystyle{\displaystyle 1\over 2}(Z_{0\bar{1}}+Z_{2\bar{1}}+Z_{\bar{1}0}+Z_{\bar{1}2}+Z_{\bar{1}\bar{1}}+Z_{\bar{1}\bar{3}})+{1\over 4}(Z_{00}+Z_{02}+Z_{20}+Z_{22})\,,

(C.16)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle{\displaystyle 1\over 2}({\mathcal{Z}}_{00}^{00}+{\mathcal{Z}}_{10}^{00}+{\mathcal{Z}}_{00}^{10}+{\mathcal{Z}}_{10}^{10})\,,

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle{\displaystyle 1\over 2}Z^{00}_{00}+{1\over 2}|W|^{2}+|W_{+}|^{2}+|W_{-}|^{2}=Z(R)\,.

Nontrivial SPT

In one case, the orbifold branch is point-wise invariant

\displaystyle Z^{A\star}_{{\mathbb{Z}}_{2}\times{\mathbb{Z}}_{2}}(R)=

\displaystyle{\displaystyle 1\over 2}(Z_{0\bar{0}}-Z_{2\bar{0}}+Z_{\bar{0}0}-Z_{\bar{0}2}+Z_{\bar{0}\bar{0}}-Z_{\bar{0}\bar{2}})+{1\over 4}(Z_{00}+Z_{02}+Z_{20}+Z_{22})\,,

(C.17)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle{\displaystyle 1\over 2}({\mathcal{Z}}_{00}^{00}+{\mathcal{Z}}_{10}^{00}+{\mathcal{Z}}_{00}^{10}+{\mathcal{Z}}_{10}^{10})-Z_{2\bar{0}}-Z_{\bar{0}2}-Z_{\bar{0}\bar{2}}\,,

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle Z^{00}_{00}(2R)-Z_{2\bar{0}}-Z_{\bar{0}2}-Z_{\bar{0}\bar{2}}\,,

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle{\displaystyle 1\over 2}({\mathcal{Z}}_{00}^{00}+|W|^{2}+|W_{+}|^{2}+|W_{-}|^{2})=Z(R)\,,

and in the other case, the orbifold branch is mapped to the unorbifolded branch with
R
doubled

\displaystyle Z^{B\star}_{{\mathbb{Z}}_{2}\times{\mathbb{Z}}_{2}}(R)=

\displaystyle{\displaystyle 1\over 2}(Z_{0\bar{1}}-Z_{2\bar{1}}+Z_{\bar{1}0}-Z_{\bar{1}2}+Z_{\bar{1}\bar{1}}-Z_{\bar{1}\bar{3}})+{1\over 4}(Z_{00}+Z_{02}+Z_{20}+Z_{22})\,,

(C.18)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle{\displaystyle 1\over 2}({\mathcal{Z}}_{00}^{00}+{\mathcal{Z}}_{10}^{00}+{\mathcal{Z}}_{00}^{10}+{\mathcal{Z}}_{10}^{10})=Z^{00}_{00}(2R)=Z_{\rm circ}(2R)\,.

C.5
D_{8}
gauging

Recall that the nonabelian
G
orbifold partition function involves sum over the conjugacy classes of
G
and in each
g
twisted sector a insertion of projector consisting of stablizers of
g
. Since
H^{2}(D_{8},U(1))={\mathbb{Z}}_{2}
, we may consider coupling to a nontrivial SPT (discrete torsion) when gauging.

Trivial SPT

\displaystyle Z_{D_{8}}=

\displaystyle{\displaystyle 1\over 8}(\sum_{a=0}^{4}Z_{0a}+\sum_{\bar{a}=0}^{4}Z_{0\bar{a}}+\sum_{a=0}^{4}Z_{2a}+\sum_{\bar{a}=0}^{4}Z_{2\bar{a}})+{1\over 4}(Z_{\bar{0}0}+Z_{\bar{0}2}+Z_{\bar{0}\bar{0}}+Z_{\bar{0}\bar{2}})

(C.19)

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle{\displaystyle 1\over 4}(Z_{\bar{1}0}+Z_{\bar{1}2}+Z_{\bar{1}\bar{1}}+Z_{\bar{1}\bar{3}})+{1\over 4}\sum_{a=0}^{4}Z_{1a}\,,

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle{\displaystyle 1\over 2}Z_{{\mathbb{Z}}_{4}}+{1\over 8}(\sum_{\bar{a}=0}^{4}Z_{0\bar{a}}+\sum_{\bar{a}=0}^{4}Z_{2\bar{a}})+{1\over 4}(Z_{\bar{0}0}+Z_{\bar{0}2}+Z_{\bar{0}\bar{0}}+Z_{\bar{0}\bar{2}})+{1\over 4}(Z_{\bar{1}0}+Z_{\bar{1}2}+Z_{\bar{1}\bar{1}}+Z_{\bar{1}\bar{3}})\,.

We can rewrite the above as a combination of partition functions from gauging subgroups of
D_{8}
,

\displaystyle Z_{D_{8}}=

\displaystyle{\displaystyle 1\over 2}Z_{{\mathbb{Z}}_{4}}+{1\over 2}Z^{A}_{{\mathbb{Z}}_{2}\times{\mathbb{Z}}_{2}}+{1\over 2}Z^{B}_{{\mathbb{Z}}_{2}\times{\mathbb{Z}}_{2}}-{1\over 2}Z^{r^{2}}_{{\mathbb{Z}}_{2}}\,.

(C.20)

Hence

\displaystyle Z_{D_{8}}(R)=Z(R)+{1\over 2}({\mathcal{Z}}_{00}^{00}({2R})-{\mathcal{Z}}_{00}^{00}({R}))=Z(2R)\,.

(C.21)

Namely, the
D_{8}
gauging preserves the orbifold branch but doubles
R
.

Nontrivial SPT

The nontrivial SPT associated with the
D_{8}
discrete torsion has nontrivial torus partition functions

\displaystyle Z^{\rm SPT}_{\bar{a}b}=i^{b}\,,~~Z^{\rm SPT}_{a\bar{b}}=i^{-a}\,,~~Z^{\rm SPT}_{\bar{a}\bar{b}}=i^{b-a}\,.

(C.22)

Consequently

\displaystyle Z^{\star}_{D_{8}}=

\displaystyle{\displaystyle 1\over 2}Z_{{\mathbb{Z}}_{4}}+{1\over 4}(Z_{0\bar{0}}-Z_{2\bar{0}}+Z_{0\bar{1}}-Z_{2\bar{1}})+{1\over 4}(Z_{\bar{0}0}-Z_{\bar{0}2}+Z_{\bar{0}\bar{0}}-Z_{\bar{0}\bar{2}})+{1\over 4}(Z_{\bar{1}0}-Z_{\bar{1}2}+Z_{\bar{1}\bar{1}}-Z_{\bar{1}\bar{3}})

(C.23)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle Z_{D_{8}}-{1\over 2}(Z_{2\bar{0}}+Z_{2\bar{1}}+Z_{\bar{0}2}+Z_{\bar{1}2}+Z_{\bar{1}\bar{3}}+Z_{\bar{0}\bar{2}})\,,

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle{\displaystyle 1\over 2}|W|^{2}+|W_{+}|^{2}+|W_{-}|^{2}+Z^{00}_{00}(R/2)=Z(R/2)\,.

Thus the
D_{8}
gauging with discrete torsion preserves the orbifold branch but halves
R
.

Appendix D
Some Lattice Defects

D.1
Ising Duality Defect

Let us use our recipe from Section
2.4
to define the Ising duality defect. We start with the Hamiltonian

H=-\sum_{j}Z_{j}Z_{j+1}+X_{j}\,,

(D.1)

(
X,Z
etc are Pauli matrices) and apply the
R_{x}=\prod_{j}X_{j}
gauging procedure

X_{j}\mapsto Z_{j}Z_{j+1}\,,

(D.2)

and

Z_{j}\mapsto\prod_{0\leq k\leq j}X_{k}\,,

(D.3)

to all
j\geq 0
. The Hamiltonian is invariant away from the resulting defect at
j=0
, but near it we have

X_{-1}\mapsto X_{-1}\,,

(D.4)

Z_{-1}Z_{0}\mapsto Z_{-1}X_{0}\,,

(D.5)

X_{0}\mapsto Z_{0}Z_{1}\,,

(D.6)

Z_{0}Z_{1}\mapsto X_{1}\,,

(D.7)

so we obtain

H^{\prime}=-\left(\sum_{j\leq-1}Z_{j-1}Z_{j}+X_{j}\right)-Z_{-1}X_{0}-\left(\sum_{j\geq 1}Z_{j-1}Z_{j}+X_{j}\right)\,,

(D.8)

in agreement with
[
38
,
9
]
.

We see that
H^{\prime}
enjoys two anti-commuting
\mathbb{Z}_{2}
symmetries,

U_{L}=\left(\prod_{j\leq-1}X_{j}\right)Z_{0}\,,

(D.9)

and

U_{R}=\left(\prod_{j\geq 0}X_{j}\right)\,,

(D.10)

which we recognize as the left and right
\mathbb{Z}_{2}
action in the duality twisted sector, with the anti-commutation reflecting the nontrivial TY F-symbol.

D.2
Continuum of topological defects in Ising
2

We begin with the XY chain

H=-\sum_{j}X_{j}X_{j+1}+Y_{j}Y_{j+1}\,.

(D.11)

This can be related to the Ising
2
chain by gauging
R_{x}=\prod_{j}X_{j}
. In terms of the operator algebra, this amounts to the transformation

X_{j}\mapsto Z_{j}Z_{j+1}\,,

(D.12)

and

Z_{j}\mapsto\prod_{k\leq j}X_{k}\,,

(D.13)

from which we obtain

H^{\prime}=-\sum_{j}Z_{j}Z_{j+2}+Z_{j}X_{j+1}Z_{j+2}\,.

(D.14)

This is related to a more familiar Hamiltonian
H^{\prime\prime}
after applying the cluster entangler transformation

X_{j}\mapsto Z_{j-1}X_{j}Z_{j+1}\,,

(D.15)

H^{\prime\prime}=-\sum_{j}Z_{j}Z_{j+2}+X_{j}\,,

(D.16)

for which the even and odd sites define two decoupled critical Ising chains.

To construct the symmetry defect for a rotation
U(\theta)=\prod_{j}e^{i\theta Z_{j}/2}
, we apply this symmetry to sites
j>0
. The bond terms are unchanged except for the 01 bond, which becomes

-\cos\theta(X_{0}X_{1}+Y_{0}Y_{1})-\sin\theta(X_{0}Y_{1}-Y_{0}X_{1})\,.

(D.17)

For
\theta\neq 0,\pi
, this defect is not invariant under the symmetry
R_{x}
, indeed
R_{x}U(\theta)R_{x}=U(-\theta)
. As in Section
2.5
, we want to replace it with a sum of defects labelled by
\theta
and
-\theta
. We do this by introducing an extra qubit at
j=1/2
, and writing the modified bond

-\cos\theta(X_{0}X_{1}+Y_{0}Y_{1})-\sin\theta(X_{0}Y_{1/2}Y_{1}-Y_{0}Y_{1/2}X_{1})\,,

(D.18)

with the new symmetry
R_{x}=X_{1/2}\prod_{j}X_{j}
.

We gauge this symmetry as above and obtain

-\cos\theta(Z_{0}Z_{1/2}Z_{1}Z_{2}+Z_{0}Y_{1/2}Z_{1}Y_{2})-\sin\theta(Z_{0}X_{1}Z_{2}+Z_{0}X_{1/2}Z_{2})\,.

(D.19)

If we then apply the cluster entangler, it becomes

-\cos\theta(Z_{0}Z_{1/2}Z_{1}Z_{2}+Y_{1/2}Z_{1}Y_{2}Z_{3})-\sin\theta(Z_{0}Z_{1/2}X_{1}+X_{1/2}Z_{1}Z_{2})\,.

(D.20)

Appendix E
Modular
S
matrices for nonabelian orbifolds

The modular S-matrices for nonabelian orbifolds of the
SU(2)_{1}
CFT were derived for
G=D_{2n}
in
[
74
]
and for
G=A_{4},S_{4},A_{5}
in
[
82
]
. Here for completeness we record their results after correcting a number of typos. As explained in Section
2.2
, they determine the Verlinde lines in these orbifold theories.

SU(2)_{1}/D_{2n}

n^{2}+7

and

. The vacuum representation corresponds to
u_{+}
. The modular
S
matrix takes slightly different forms for
n
even versus for
n
odd and are given in Table
9
and Table
10
respectively. Here
\sigma_{rs}=2{\delta}_{rs}-1
.
